# Brass: Birmingham

> Chinese name: **工业革命 Brass：伯明翰** · Doc #79 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../79_%E5%B7%A5%E4%B8%9A%E9%9D%A9%E5%91%BD_Brass.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/79)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 2–4 (best: 3-4) |
| Play time | 60–120 min |
| Age | 14+ |
| Complexity | ●●●●○ Medium-heavy (4/5) |
| Tags | economic, network, heavy, hand management |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** none on the photo — inferred area: ≈ top of the D/E "free to borrow" cabinet; ≈ top of the E–G cabinets (above the E1/E2, F1/F2, G1/G2 signs) *(inferred)*
- **Photos:** [155102](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155102.jpg), [155114](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155114.jpg), [155121](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155121.jpg)
- **Close-up crops:** [c5](../../photos/crops/c5_155102_top.jpg), [c6](../../photos/crops/c6_155114_top.jpg), [c7](../../photos/crops/c7_155121_top.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 第三轮照片 155102：D/E “免费借玩”货架顶部大盒堆 → 右上
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155114 / 155121：顶部大盒堆 → ★ 工业都市：伯明翰 Brass: Birmingham（确认是伯明翰）

## Overview

The Industrial Revolution over the canal and rail eras: build industries, links and sell goods using loans.

## Setup

1. Set up the market, merchants and cards; each player has a board and money.

## How to play

1. Two actions per turn (one in the first): build, network, develop, sell, loan, scout.
2. Coal and iron consumption follow connection rules.
3. Turn order: least money spent goes first.

## Game end & scoring

- After each era score links and flipped industries; the canal era removes canals and level-1 industries; most points wins.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** After each era score links and flipped industries; the canal era removes canals and level-1 industries; most points wins.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Two actions per turn (one in the first): build, network, develop, sell, loan, scout.
3. **Setup check:** Set up the market, merchants and cards; each player has a board and money.
4. **The one rule people forget:** Loans reduce income level.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Cotton and pottery need merchants to flip.
- Use others' coal and iron.
- Claim key routes in the canal era.

## Common mistakes

- Loans reduce income level.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** experienced gamers who want a deep think
- **Not ideal for:** relaxed chatting sessions

## Detailed supplement: components, worked example, scoring, FAQ, player counts, variants

> Written by the editor from the rules above as a walkthrough; numbers in the example are illustrative. The rulebook in the box always wins.

### Component checklist

- [ ] No complete public component list — use the list in the box.

Before you start:
- Count the components against the rulebook list; sort cards by type — a missing card can break the balance.
- Check tokens, dice and score markers; coins or generic tokens can stand in for missing pieces.
- Hand out reference cards before the first game to save rulebook time.

### Worked example (illustrative)

1. Canal era: I build a cotton mill in Birmingham.
2. Pay money and connected coal.
3. I build a canal link.
4. At era end flipped industries and links score.

### Scoring example

- After the rail era score industries and links.

### Rules FAQ / rulings

- **Q: Loans?**  
  A: Take £30, income drops 3 levels.

### Player-count differences

- 2–4.
- Best with: 3-4 players.

### Official variants & expansions

- See rulebook.

## Advanced guide: teaching script, time budget, phase strategy, extra FAQ

> Summarised by the editor from the rules above for teaching and improving; the rulebook wins on conflicts.

### First-game time budget

- Teaching: about 25 min (weight ●●●●).
- Play: 60–120 min; budget about 145 min for a first game.
- Total: about 170 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> British Industrial Revolution in canal and rail eras. Build industries, links, sell goods, take loans. Networks drive scoring.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Build networks in the canal era.
- **Mid:** Use loans wisely.
- **Late:** Upgrade in the rail era.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: End of canal era?**  
  A: Level-1 industries and canals are removed.
- **Q: Repay loans?**  
  A: No, income drops.

# Night of the Ninja

> Chinese name: **忍者之夜** · Doc #80 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../80_%E5%BF%8D%E8%80%85%E4%B9%8B%E5%A4%9C_Night_of_the_Ninja.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/80)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 4–11 (best: 6-8) |
| Play time | 15–30 min |
| Age | 12+ |
| Complexity | ●●○○○ Light (2/5) |
| Tags | teams, deduction, drafting, hidden role |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** D1 / D2 / E1 / E2 / F2
- **Photos:** [155102](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155102.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 第三轮照片 155102：D1/D2 层（标签 E1 / E2 / F2） → 忍者之夜 Night of the Ninja

## Overview

Lotus and Crane clans assassinate each other: draft ninja cards, act in the night, then reveal.

## Setup

1. Take clan cards by player count; each player draws a secret clan card and 3 ninja cards.

## How to play

1. Draft: keep 1, pass 2; repeat.
2. Night: resolve in card number order (spy, mystic, trickster, assassin…).
3. Reveal and score.

## Game end & scoring

- Clan scores when the opponents' highest-ranked member is killed; the Ronin and others have own goals; play to the point target.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** Clan scores when the opponents' highest-ranked member is killed; the Ronin and others have own goals; play to the point target.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Draft: keep 1, pass 2; repeat.
3. **Setup check:** Take clan cards by player count; each player draws a secret clan card and 3 ninja cards.
4. **The one rule people forget:** Odd counts add the Ronin.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- What you pass is information.
- Reveal identities selectively.
- Assassinate the top-ranked enemy.

## Common mistakes

- Odd counts add the Ronin.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** big parties; people who love deduction and guessing identities
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

## Detailed supplement: components, worked example, scoring, FAQ, player counts, variants

> Written by the editor from the rules above as a walkthrough; numbers in the example are illustrative. The rulebook in the box always wins.

### Component checklist

From the Chinese doc (original wording):

- [ ] 流派牌（**莲花**、**仙鹤**两派，各有 1、2、3… 号等级；奇数人数加入**浪人**）、忍者牌、忍者立牌、35 个手里剑荣誉标记（背面藏分值，放布袋）。

Before you start:
- Count the components against the rulebook list; sort cards by type — a missing card can break the balance.
- Check tokens, dice and score markers; coins or generic tokens can stand in for missing pieces.
- Hand out reference cards before the first game to save rulebook time.

### Worked example (illustrative)

1. 6p: secret clan cards, 3 ninja cards each.
2. Draft: keep 1 pass 2, keep 1 more.
3. Night: Spy phase I look at B; Tricksters swap.
4. Shinobi phase C checks me and kills me.
5. Reveal: compare the lowest surviving rank; Lotus wins and all Lotus members draw honour tokens.

### Scoring example

- Winning clan members draw hidden honour tokens; game ends at 10+.

### Rules FAQ / rulings

- **Q: Can dead players act?**  
  A: No, except reaction cards when killed.
- **Q: Can I show cards?**  
  A: No, but discussion is free.

### Player-count differences

- 4–11; odd counts add a Ronin; 2p mode exists.
- Best with: 6-8 players.

### Official variants & expansions

- See rulebook.

## Advanced guide: teaching script, time budget, phase strategy, extra FAQ

> Summarised by the editor from the rules above for teaching and improving; the rulebook wins on conflicts.

### First-game time budget

- Teaching: about 10 min (weight ●●).
- Play: 15–30 min; budget about 35 min for a first game.
- Total: about 45 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> Lotus and Crane clans assassinate each other. Draft ninja cards, act at night in order: peek, swap, kill. Reveal at dawn.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Draft info cards.
- **Mid:** Read identities at night.
- **Late:** Kill key targets.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: Multiple rounds?**  
  A: Three, by honour.
- **Q: Ronin win?**  
  A: See rulebook.

# Decrypto

> Chinese name: **截码战 Decrypto** · Doc #81 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../81_%E6%88%AA%E7%A0%81%E6%88%98_Decrypto.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/81)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 3–8 (best: 6-8) |
| Play time | 15–45 min |
| Age | 12+ |
| Complexity | ●●○○○ Light (2/5) |
| Tags | team, word, deduction, association |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** D1 / D2 / E1 / E2 / F2
- **Photos:** [155102](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155102.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 第三轮照片 155102：D1/D2 层（标签 E1 / E2 / F2） → 截码战 Decrypto

## Overview

Two teams each have 4 secret keywords and transmit 3-digit codes with clues; the other team tries to intercept.

## Setup

1. Two screens; each team places 4 keyword cards in slots 1–4 visible only to them.

## How to play

1. An encryptor draws a code (e.g. 3-1-4) and gives 3 clues.
2. Their team guesses the code; the opponents try to intercept (not in round 1).
3. Miscommunication earns a white token; interceptions earn black tokens.

## Game end & scoring

- 2 interceptions win; 2 miscommunications lose; tiebreaks per rules after 8 rounds.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** 2 interceptions win; 2 miscommunications lose; tiebreaks per rules after 8 rounds.
2. **What you do on a turn:** An encryptor draws a code (e.g. 3-1-4) and gives 3 clues.
3. **Setup check:** Two screens; each team places 4 keyword cards in slots 1–4 visible only to them.
4. **The one rule people forget:** No interceptions in round 1.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Use inside jokes only your team knows.
- Vary association types.
- Log the opponents' clues per number.

## Common mistakes

- No interceptions in round 1.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** big parties; people who love deduction and guessing identities
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

## Detailed supplement: components, worked example, scoring, FAQ, player counts, variants

> Written by the editor from the rules above as a walkthrough; numbers in the example are illustrative. The rulebook in the box always wins.

### Component checklist

From the Chinese doc (original wording):

- [ ] 黑白两色屏风各 1、关键词卡 110 张、黑白密码卡各 24 张、拦截标记（白）4、误码标记（黑）4、沙漏、记录纸。

Before you start:
- Count the components against the rulebook list; sort cards by type — a missing card can break the balance.
- Check tokens, dice and score markers; coins or generic tokens can stand in for missing pieces.
- Hand out reference cards before the first game to save rulebook time.

### Worked example (illustrative)

1. Two teams; each has 4 secret keywords numbered 1–4.
2. Our encryptor draws code 3-1-4 and gives three clues.
3. My teammates decode 3-1-4 correctly while the opponents try to intercept.
4. Using past clue history, the opponents intercept and gain an interception token.
5. 2 interceptions wins; 2 miscommunications loses.

### Scoring example

- 2 interceptions win, 2 miscommunications lose; after 8 rounds compare per rulebook.

### Rules FAQ / rulings

- **Q: Do we intercept in round 1?**  
  A: Usually not (see rulebook).
- **Q: Can clues reference positions?**  
  A: No, follow the rulebook clue restrictions.

### Player-count differences

- 3–8; 3p uses a variant.
- Best with: 6-8 players.

### Official variants & expansions

- See rulebook.

## Advanced guide: teaching script, time budget, phase strategy, extra FAQ

> Summarised by the editor from the rules above for teaching and improving; the rulebook wins on conflicts.

### First-game time budget

- Teaching: about 10 min (weight ●●).
- Play: 15–45 min; budget about 55 min for a first game.
- Total: about 65 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> Each team has 4 secret keywords numbered 1–4. Encryptor draws a 3-digit code and gives three clues. Teammates decode; opponents try to intercept from clue history. Two intercepts win; two miscommunications lose.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Keep clues subtle early.
- **Mid:** Log opponents' clues.
- **Late:** Vary angles later.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: Multilingual clues?**  
  A: Agree beforehand.
- **Q: Clue count?**  
  A: One per digit.

# Ubongo 3D

> Chinese name: **乌邦果 3D** · Doc #82 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../82_%E4%B9%8C%E9%82%A6%E6%9E%9C3D_Ubongo_3D.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/82)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 1–4 (best: 2-4) |
| Play time | 15–30 min |
| Age | 8+ |
| Complexity | ●●○○○ Light (2/5) |
| Tags | real-time, spatial, puzzle, race, family |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** D1 / D2 / E1 / E2 / F2
- **Photos:** [155102](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155102.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 第三轮照片 155102：D1/D2 层（标签 E1 / E2 / F2） → 乌邦果 3D Ubongo 3D

## Overview

3D Ubongo: when the timer starts, everyone builds a two-level shape with the given pieces; finishers take gems.

## Setup

1. Puzzle boards (3-piece and 4-piece sides) in stacks; each player has pieces.

## How to play

1. Roll or select pieces; flip the timer; fill your board exactly two levels high.
2. Call Ubongo when done; take gems in order.

## Game end & scoring

- After 9 rounds, most gems of one colour (or per rulebook) wins.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** After 9 rounds, most gems of one colour (or per rulebook) wins.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Roll or select pieces; flip the timer; fill your board exactly two levels high.
3. **Setup check:** Puzzle boards (3-piece and 4-piece sides) in stacks; each player has pieces.
4. **The one rule people forget:** Exactly two levels, no holes or overhang.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Place the largest or oddest piece first.
- Both levels must be complete.
- Start with 3-piece sides.

## Common mistakes

- Exactly two levels, no holes or overhang.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** fans of speed and excitement
- **Not ideal for:** people who want to think slowly and quietly

## Detailed supplement: components, worked example, scoring, FAQ, player counts, variants

> Written by the editor from the rules above as a walkthrough; numbers in the example are illustrative. The rulebook in the box always wins.

### Component checklist

From the Chinese doc (original wording):

- [ ] 36 张题板（每张双面：一面用 3 块、一面用 4 块，难度不同）、每人一套立体多格块（共 40 块）、骰子、沙漏、宝石（红 4 分、蓝 3 分、绿 2 分、棕 1 分）和布袋。

Before you start:
- Count the components against the rulebook list; sort cards by type — a missing card can break the balance.
- Check tokens, dice and score markers; coins or generic tokens can stand in for missing pieces.
- Hand out reference cards before the first game to save rulebook time.

### Worked example (illustrative)

1. Everyone has the same 3D pieces and a puzzle card.
2. Roll to choose which pieces to use; flip the timer.
3. Build the shape covering the area exactly at the required height.
4. First to finish shouts 'Ubongo' and takes gems; others finishing in time also take.
5. Most gem value after the rounds wins.

### Scoring example

- After 9 rounds total gem values (red 4, blue 3, green 2, brown 1).

### Rules FAQ / rulings

- **Q: Can pieces overhang?**  
  A: No.

### Player-count differences

- 1–4.
- Best with: 2-4 players.

### Official variants & expansions

- Easy/hard puzzle sides.

## Advanced guide: teaching script, time budget, phase strategy, extra FAQ

> Summarised by the editor from the rules above for teaching and improving; the rulebook wins on conflicts.

### First-game time budget

- Teaching: about 10 min (weight ●●).
- Play: 15–30 min; budget about 35 min for a first game.
- Total: about 45 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> Everyone has identical 3D pieces and a puzzle board. Roll to pick the piece set, flip the timer. Build simultaneously: cover the white area exactly, exactly 2 layers high. First shouts 'Ubongo' and takes a blue gem, second takes brown; everyone in time draws one from the bag. Highest gem value after 9 rounds wins.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Warm up on the easy side.
- **Mid:** Place the oddest piece first.
- **Late:** Finish in time to secure bag draws.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: Gem values?**  
  A: Red 4, blue 3, green 2, brown 1.
- **Q: Overhangs allowed?**  
  A: No — exact coverage, 2 layers.

---

# Tiny Towns

> Chinese name: **小小城镇 Tiny Towns** · Doc #83 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../83_%E5%B0%8F%E5%B0%8F%E5%9F%8E%E9%95%87_Tiny_Towns.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/83)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 1–6 (best: 2-4) |
| Play time | 45–60 min |
| Age | 14+ |
| Complexity | ●●●○○ Medium (3/5) |
| Tags | pattern building, resources, simultaneous, spatial, strategy |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** D1 / D2 / E1 / E2 / F2 / F3
- **Photos:** [155102](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155102.jpg), [155114](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155114.jpg), [155121](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155121.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 第三轮照片 155102：D1/D2 层（标签 E1 / E2 / F2） → 小小城镇 Tiny Towns
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155114 / 155121：F2 / F3 行 → ★ 现代艺术、★ 山屋惊魂、★ 出包魔法师、★ 小小城镇、★ 只言片语 ×2、★ 卡卡颂 ×2+、★ 拉斯维加斯 豪华版 + 普通版

## Overview

Each round someone names a resource and everyone must place it in their 4×4 town; build patterns into buildings until you run out of space.

## Setup

1. Each player has a 4×4 board; Cottage plus 6 random building types are used.

## How to play

1. The master builder names a resource; everyone places it.
2. Resources matching a building pattern may be replaced by that building.
3. Monuments are personal buildings.

## Game end & scoring

- When a player cannot place, they are out; score buildings, −1 per empty square.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** When a player cannot place, they are out; score buildings, −1 per empty square.
2. **What you do on a turn:** The master builder names a resource; everyone places it.
3. **Setup check:** Each player has a 4×4 board; Cottage plus 6 random building types are used.
4. **The one rule people forget:** Placed resources cannot move.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Name resources hardest for others.
- Overlap patterns to save squares.
- Feed cottages.

## Common mistakes

- Placed resources cannot move.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** most board gamers
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

## Detailed supplement: components, worked example, scoring, FAQ, player counts, variants

> Written by the editor from the rules above as a walkthrough; numbers in the example are illustrative. The rulebook in the box always wins.

### Component checklist

- [ ] No complete public component list — use the list in the box.

Before you start:
- Count the components against the rulebook list; sort cards by type — a missing card can break the balance.
- Check tokens, dice and score markers; coins or generic tokens can stand in for missing pieces.
- Hand out reference cards before the first game to save rulebook time.

### Worked example (illustrative)

1. Three building cards are revealed.
2. A is master builder and names Brick; everyone places brick on their 4x4 grid.
3. I name Wood and complete a cottage pattern.
4. I remove the resources and place a cottage.
5. When no one can place, score.

### Scoring example

- Score each building; −1 per empty square.

### Rules FAQ / rulings

- **Q: Can I refuse a resource?**  
  A: No; if you can't place it you're done.

### Player-count differences

- 1–6.
- Best with: 2-4 players.

### Official variants & expansions

- Solo mode; expansions.

## Advanced guide: teaching script, time budget, phase strategy, extra FAQ

> Summarised by the editor from the rules above for teaching and improving; the rulebook wins on conflicts.

### First-game time budget

- Teaching: about 15 min (weight ●●●).
- Play: 45–60 min; budget about 70 min for a first game.
- Total: about 85 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> The master builder names a resource; everyone places it in their 4x4 town. Arrange resources in a building's pattern to replace them with that building. When your town is full you're done. Score buildings; −1 per empty square.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Plan building spots.
- **Mid:** Name resources that hurt others.
- **Late:** Fill every square late.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: Can I refuse a resource?**  
  A: No; if you can't place, you're done.
- **Q: Can buildings be removed?**  
  A: No.

---

# Dixit (with Stella)

> Chinese name: **只言片语 Dixit** · Doc #84 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../84_%E5%8F%AA%E8%A8%80%E7%89%87%E8%AF%AD_Dixit_%E5%90%AB%E6%98%9F%E7%89%A9%E8%AF%AD.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/84)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 3–8 (best: 5-6) |
| Play time | 30 min |
| Age | 8+ |
| Complexity | ●○○○○ Very light (1/5) |
| Tags | party, association, art, family, classic |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** D1 / D2 / E1 / E2 / F2 / F3 / F5 / G3
- **Photos:** [155102](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155102.jpg), [155114](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155114.jpg), [155121](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155121.jpg), [155125](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155125.jpg), [155128](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155128.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 第三轮照片 155102：D1/D2 层（标签 E1 / E2 / F2） → 只言片语 Dixit + 星物语（同类说书人玩法，合为一份文档）
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155114 / 155121：F2 / F3 行 → ★ 现代艺术、★ 山屋惊魂、★ 出包魔法师、★ 小小城镇、★ 只言片语 ×2、★ 卡卡颂 ×2+、★ 拉斯维加斯 豪华版 + 普通版
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155125：F3 / G3 层（与第四轮重叠） → ★ 犯罪现场（千骐 / 千智）、★ 李智慧生存游戏、★ 以鹅传鹅 ×2、★ 只言片语、★ 浮生青云录
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155128：F5 层透明收纳箱（蓝框编号标签） → 14 只言片语

## Overview

A storyteller gives a clue for a picture; others add matching pictures; everyone votes for the storyteller's card.

## Setup

1. Deal 6 picture cards each; rabbits on the score track; voting tokens.

## How to play

1. The storyteller picks a card and gives a clue (word, sentence, sound).
2. Others give a matching card face down; shuffle and reveal.
3. Everyone except the storyteller votes.

## Game end & scoring

- If all or nobody find the storyteller's card, others score 2 and the storyteller 0; otherwise storyteller and correct voters score 3; extra votes on your card score 1.
- Play to 30 points or until the deck runs out.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** If all or nobody find the storyteller's card, others score 2 and the storyteller 0; otherwise storyteller and correct voters score 3; extra votes on your card score 1.
2. **What you do on a turn:** The storyteller picks a card and gives a clue (word, sentence, sound).
3. **Setup check:** Deal 6 picture cards each; rabbits on the score track; voting tokens.
4. **The one rule people forget:** You cannot vote for your own card.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Clues should be partially obvious.
- Choose decoys that draw votes.
- Songs, poems and sounds work.

## Common mistakes

- You cannot vote for your own card.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** big parties; icebreakers; newcomers and grandparents can learn it instantly
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

## Detailed supplement: components, worked example, scoring, FAQ, player counts, variants

> Written by the editor from the rules above as a walkthrough; numbers in the example are illustrative. The rulebook in the box always wins.

### Component checklist

- [ ] No complete public component list — use the list in the box.

Before you start:
- Count the components against the rulebook list; sort cards by type — a missing card can break the balance.
- Check tokens, dice and score markers; coins or generic tokens can stand in for missing pieces.
- Hand out reference cards before the first game to save rulebook time.

### Worked example (illustrative)

1. The storyteller picks a card and gives a clue.
2. Others pick their best-fitting card.
3. Shuffle, reveal and vote.
4. Some but not all guess right: storyteller and guessers +3; other card owners +1 per vote.
5. All or none guess right: storyteller 0, others +2.

### Scoring example

- First to 30.

### Rules FAQ / rulings

- **Q: Does the storyteller vote?**  
  A: No.

### Player-count differences

- 3–8.
- Best with: 5-6 players.

### Official variants & expansions

- Many expansion decks.

## Advanced guide: teaching script, time budget, phase strategy, extra FAQ

> Summarised by the editor from the rules above for teaching and improving; the rulebook wins on conflicts.

### First-game time budget

- Teaching: about 5 min (weight ●).
- Play: 30–30 min; budget about 35 min for a first game.
- Total: about 40 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> Storyteller picks a card and gives a clue. Others give their best match; shuffle, reveal and vote. Some correct: storyteller and correct guessers +3, owners +1 per vote. All or none: storyteller 0, others +2.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Clues should be neither too obvious nor obscure.
- **Mid:** Match the clue's mood.
- **Late:** Maximise your storyteller turns late.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: Does the storyteller vote?**  
  A: No.
- **Q: Can I vote for my own?**  
  A: No.

# Modern Art

> Chinese name: **现代艺术 Modern Art** · Doc #85 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../85_%E7%8E%B0%E4%BB%A3%E8%89%BA%E6%9C%AF_Modern_Art.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/85)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 3–5 (best: 4-5) |
| Play time | 45 min |
| Age | 10+ |
| Complexity | ●●●○○ Medium (3/5) |
| Tags | auction, economic, market manipulation, strategy |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** D1 / D2 / E1 / E2 / F2 / F3
- **Photos:** [155102](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155102.jpg), [155114](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155114.jpg), [155121](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155121.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 第三轮照片 155102：D1/D2 层（标签 E1 / E2 / F2） → 现代艺术 Modern Art
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155114 / 155121：F2 / F3 行 → ★ 现代艺术、★ 山屋惊魂、★ 出包魔法师、★ 小小城镇、★ 只言片语 ×2、★ 卡卡颂 ×2+、★ 拉斯维加斯 豪华版 + 普通版

## Overview

Knizia's auction classic: auction paintings in five formats and drive artists' values; only top artists each season pay.

## Setup

1. Each player has $100 behind a screen; deal painting cards (5 artists).

## How to play

1. Take turns as auctioneer, playing a painting with its auction type: open, once around, sealed, fixed price, or double.
2. Money goes to the auctioneer (or to the bank if the auctioneer buys).
3. A season ends when an artist's 5th painting is played.

## Game end & scoring

- Top three artists score 30/20/10 cumulatively; sell paintings to the bank; after 4 seasons most money wins.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** Top three artists score 30/20/10 cumulatively; sell paintings to the bank; after 4 seasons most money wins.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Take turns as auctioneer, playing a painting with its auction type: open, once around, sealed, fixed price, or double.
3. **Setup check:** Each player has $100 behind a screen; deal painting cards (5 artists).
4. **The one rule people forget:** Only top-three artists of the season have value.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Sell high to others.
- Promote artists you own.
- Final-season paintings are worth most.

## Common mistakes

- Only top-three artists of the season have value.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** most board gamers
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

## Detailed supplement: components, worked example, scoring, FAQ, player counts, variants

> Written by the editor from the rules above as a walkthrough; numbers in the example are illustrative. The rulebook in the box always wins.

### Component checklist

- [ ] No complete public component list — use the list in the box.

Before you start:
- Count the components against the rulebook list; sort cards by type — a missing card can break the balance.
- Check tokens, dice and score markers; coins or generic tokens can stand in for missing pieces.
- Hand out reference cards before the first game to save rulebook time.

### Worked example (illustrative)

1. I play an artist card and announce the auction type.
2. A wins and pays me.
3. When an artist's 5th card appears, the round ends.
4. Top three artists are worth 30/20/10 and accumulate over rounds.
5. Most money after 4 rounds wins.

### Scoring example

- Paintings sell for accumulated artist values; most money wins.

### Rules FAQ / rulings

- **Q: If I buy my own painting?**  
  A: Pay the bank.

### Player-count differences

- 3–5.
- Best with: 4-5 players.

### Official variants & expansions

- Several editions.

## Advanced guide: teaching script, time budget, phase strategy, extra FAQ

> Summarised by the editor from the rules above for teaching and improving; the rulebook wins on conflicts.

### First-game time budget

- Teaching: about 15 min (weight ●●●).
- Play: 45–45 min; budget about 55 min for a first game.
- Total: about 70 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> Auction paintings using the method on each card: open, once-around, sealed, fixed price, double. The buyer pays the seller (or the bank if you buy your own). The 5th card of an artist ends the round. Rank artists: 30/20/10, cumulative. Sell to the bank; most cash after 4 rounds wins.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Read which artists others hold.
- **Mid:** Manipulate with doubles and sealed bids.
- **Late:** Concentrate money on top artists in round 4.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: Is the 5th card auctioned?**  
  A: No.
- **Q: Are artists outside top 3 worth anything?**  
  A: Not this round.

# Betrayal at House on the Hill (2nd Ed.)

> Chinese name: **山屋惊魂第二版** · Doc #86 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../86_%E5%B1%B1%E5%B1%8B%E6%83%8A%E9%AD%82%E7%AC%AC%E4%BA%8C%E7%89%88_Betrayal_at_House_on_the_Hill.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/86)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 3–6 (best: 5) |
| Play time | 60 min |
| Age | 12+ |
| Complexity | ●●●○○ Medium (3/5) |
| Tags | horror, exploration, traitor, story, semi-co-op |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** D1 / D2 / E1 / E2 / F2 / F3
- **Photos:** [155102](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155102.jpg), [155114](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155114.jpg), [155121](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155121.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 第三轮照片 155102：D1/D2 层（标签 E1 / E2 / F2） → 山屋惊魂 第二版 Betrayal at House on the Hill
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155114 / 155121：F2 / F3 行 → ★ 现代艺术、★ 山屋惊魂、★ 出包魔法师、★ 小小城镇、★ 只言片语 ×2、★ 卡卡颂 ×2+、★ 拉斯维加斯 豪华版 + 普通版

## Overview

Explore a haunted house; at some point the Haunt begins, one player becomes the traitor, and both sides follow a secret scenario.

## Setup

1. Choose explorers (4 traits); place starting tiles.

## How to play

1. Move by speed, discover rooms, draw events, items or omens.
2. After each omen, roll a haunt check.
3. When the haunt starts, look up the scenario; the traitor leaves to read their book.

## Game end & scoring

- Each scenario defines win conditions for heroes and traitor.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** Each scenario defines win conditions for heroes and traitor.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Move by speed, discover rooms, draw events, items or omens.
3. **Setup check:** Choose explorers (4 traits); place starting tiles.
4. **The one rule people forget:** Haunt rolls grow with omens.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Collect items before the haunt.
- Traitors learn rules quickly; heroes plan together.
- Enjoy the story.

## Common mistakes

- Haunt rolls grow with omens.
- Scenario numbers differ by edition.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** most board gamers
- **Not ideal for:** players who dislike conflict or backstabbing

## Detailed supplement: components, worked example, scoring, FAQ, player counts, variants

> Written by the editor from the rules above as a walkthrough; numbers in the example are illustrative. The rulebook in the box always wins.

### Component checklist

From the Chinese doc (original wording):

- [ ] 房间板块（地下室 / 一楼 / 楼上）、6 个探险者角色卡（四项属性：速度、力量、理智、知识）、事件卡、物品卡、预兆卡、8 颗特制骰（每面 0/1/2）、两本剧本书：**《背叛者之书》**（Traitor's Tome）和**《幸存者秘籍》**（Secrets of Survival），各 50 个“作祟”剧本。

Before you start:
- Count the components against the rulebook list; sort cards by type — a missing card can break the balance.
- Check tokens, dice and score markers; coins or generic tokens can stand in for missing pieces.
- Hand out reference cards before the first game to save rulebook time.

### Worked example (illustrative)

1. Explorers start in the entrance hall; I open a door and draw a room.
2. An omen symbol: draw an omen and roll 6 dice for the haunt.
3. The roll is under the omen count — the haunt begins.
4. Look up the haunt and traitor; the traitor leaves to read their book.
5. Each side pursues their goal.

### Scoring example

- Win per the haunt scenario.

### Rules FAQ / rulings

- **Q: How many dice for haunt rolls?**  
  A: Six dice; trigger if below the omen count.

### Player-count differences

- 3–6.
- Best with: 5 players.

### Official variants & expansions

- Widow's Walk; 3rd ed differs.

## Advanced guide: teaching script, time budget, phase strategy, extra FAQ

> Summarised by the editor from the rules above for teaching and improving; the rulebook wins on conflicts.

### First-game time budget

- Teaching: about 15 min (weight ●●●).
- Play: 60–60 min; budget about 70 min for a first game.
- Total: about 85 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> Explorers start in the entrance hall. Opening unexplored doors reveals rooms. Omen symbols draw omen cards and trigger haunt rolls: roll 6 dice; if the total is less than the omen count, the haunt begins. Look up the scenario and traitor; each side reads its book and pursues its goal.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Gather items and boost traits early.
- **Mid:** Read your book separately after the haunt.
- **Late:** Focus on the scenario objective.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: How many dice for haunt rolls?**  
  A: Six; trigger if below the omen count.
- **Q: Trait to skull before the haunt?**  
  A: You can't die before the haunt.

# Abracada…What!

> Chinese name: **出包魔法师** · Doc #87 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../87_%E5%87%BA%E5%8C%85%E9%AD%94%E6%B3%95%E5%B8%88_Abracada_What.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/87)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 2–5 (best: 3-4) |
| Play time | 20–30 min |
| Age | 8+ |
| Complexity | ●●○○○ Light (2/5) |
| Tags | deduction, dice, Indian poker, family |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** D1 / D2 / E1 / E2 / F2 / F3
- **Photos:** [155056](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155056.jpg), [155102](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155102.jpg), [155114](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155114.jpg), [155121](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155121.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 第三轮照片 155102：D1/D2 层（标签 E1 / E2 / F2） → 出包魔法师 Abracada…What!（155056 还有一张散落的卡片/说明书）
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155114 / 155121：F2 / F3 行 → ★ 现代艺术、★ 山屋惊魂、★ 出包魔法师、★ 小小城镇、★ 只言片语 ×2、★ 卡卡颂 ×2+、★ 拉斯维加斯 豪华版 + 普通版

## Overview

See others' spell stones but not your own: announce spells to cast; wrong guesses hurt you.

## Setup

1. Distribute spell stones (1–8) face outward so you cannot see yours.

## How to play

1. Announce a spell number; if you have it, it takes effect; otherwise lose life by die roll.
2. Each subsequent spell in a turn must be equal or higher.

## Game end & scoring

- A round ends when someone casts all their stones or reduces an opponent to 0 life; score on the tower (winner and survivors differ, see rulebook).
- First to 8 points wins.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** A round ends when someone casts all their stones or reduces an opponent to 0 life; score on the tower (winner and survivors differ, see rulebook).
2. **What you do on a turn:** Announce a spell number; if you have it, it takes effect; otherwise lose life by die roll.
3. **Setup check:** Distribute spell stones (1–8) face outward so you cannot see yours.
4. **The one rule people forget:** Spell numbers cannot decrease within a turn.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Count visible spells to deduce yours.
- Try low numbers first.
- The owl (8) lets you peek at secret stones.

## Common mistakes

- Spell numbers cannot decrease within a turn.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** people who love deduction and guessing identities
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

Before you start:
- Count the components against the rulebook list; sort cards by type — a missing card can break the balance.
- Check tokens, dice and score markers; coins or generic tokens can stand in for missing pieces.
- Hand out reference cards before the first game to save rulebook time.

### Worked example (illustrative)

1. Five spell stones facing outward; you can't see your own.
2. I guess I have spell 3 and cast it.
3. Wrong guess costs me a life.
4. A player hits 0 and the round ends.
5. First to 8 points wins.

### Scoring example

- Score per rulebook.

### Rules FAQ / rulings

- **Q: Can I see my stones?**  
  A: No.

### Player-count differences

- 2–5.
- Best with: 3-4 players.

### Official variants & expansions

- See rulebook.

## Advanced guide: teaching script, time budget, phase strategy, extra FAQ

> Summarised by the editor from the rules above for teaching and improving; the rulebook wins on conflicts.

### First-game time budget

- Teaching: about 10 min (weight ●●).
- Play: 20–30 min; budget about 35 min for a first game.
- Total: about 45 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> Five spell stones face outward — you can't see your own. Name a spell number; if you have it, cast it; if not, lose life. The round ends when someone hits 0 life or empties their stones.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Call low numbers first — there are more of them.
- **Mid:** Deduce from visible and used stones.
- **Late:** Play safe when low on life.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: Can I see my stones?**  
  A: No.
- **Q: Where do cast stones go?**  
  A: Face up in the centre.

# Monopoly World Tour (Silver)

> Chinese name: **大富翁世界之旅（银牌）** · Doc #88 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../88_%E5%A4%A7%E5%AF%8C%E7%BF%81%E4%B8%96%E7%95%8C%E4%B9%8B%E6%97%85%E9%93%B6%E7%89%8C_Uncle_Wang.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/88)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 2–6 (best: 4) |
| Play time | 60–180 min |
| Age | 8+ |
| Complexity | ●○○○○ Very light (1/5) |
| Tags | classic, dice rolling, economic, family |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** D3 / E3
- **Photos:** [155102](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155102.jpg), [155106](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155106.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 第三轮照片 155102 / 155106：D3 层与 E3 层 → 大富翁 世界之旅 银牌（银行游戏盘）

## Overview

Roll around the board, buy cities, build and collect rent; chance cards add twists.

## Setup

1. Choose a banker; give equal starting money (often 1500).

## How to play

1. Roll and move; collect salary passing GO.
2. Buy unowned property or auction it; pay rent on owned property.
3. Build houses with full colour sets; chance, fate and jail rules.

## Game end & scoring

- Last solvent player wins, or richest at a time limit.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** Last solvent player wins, or richest at a time limit.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Roll and move; collect salary passing GO.
3. **Setup check:** Choose a banker; give equal starting money (often 1500).
4. **The one rule people forget:** Amounts vary by edition.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Complete colour sets.
- Cash flow matters.
- Agree a time limit.

## Common mistakes

- Amounts vary by edition.
- Unbought properties go to auction (official rule).

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** icebreakers; newcomers and grandparents can learn it instantly
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

## Detailed supplement: components, worked example, scoring, FAQ, player counts, variants

> Written by the editor from the rules above as a walkthrough; numbers in the example are illustrative. The rulebook in the box always wins.

> Chinese name: **熊熊公园 Bärenpark** · Doc #90 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../90_%E7%86%8A%E7%86%8A%E5%85%AC%E5%9B%AD_Barenpark.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/90)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |

## Where is it on the shelf?

## Overview

Build your bear park with polyomino tiles: covering icons grants new tiles and park sections; early bear enclosures score more.

## Setup

1. Each player starts with a 4×4 park section; central supply of tiles.

## How to play

1. Place a tile; icons covered give rewards (green areas, animal houses, enclosures, new sections).
2. Completed sections earn bear statues.

## Game end & scoring

- When a player completes their park, finish the round; score tiles and statues.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** When a player completes their park, finish the round; score tiles and statues.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Place a tile; icons covered give rewards (green areas, animal houses, enclosures, new sections).
3. **Setup check:** Each player starts with a 4×4 park section; central supply of tiles.
4. **The one rule people forget:** New sections must be adjacent.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Grab high-value enclosures early.
- Plan section entrances.
- Statues score by completion order.

## Common mistakes

- New sections must be adjacent.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** most board gamers
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

# Sushi Express

> Chinese name: **寿司快递** · Doc #91 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../91_%E5%AF%BF%E5%8F%B8%E5%BF%AB%E9%80%92_Sushi_Express.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/91)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 3–6 (best: 4) |
| Play time | 30 min |
| Age | 8+ |
| Complexity | ●●○○○ Light (2/5) |
| Tags | dice, betting, push-your-luck, family |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** D3 / E3
- **Photos:** [155102](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155102.jpg), [155106](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155106.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 第三轮照片 155102 / 155106：D3 层与 E3 层 → 寿司快递 Sushi Express

## Overview

Michael Schacht's betting-and-dice delivery race: bet a number 2–12, the highest bidder must roll it on 2 dice, then deliver sushi around a ring to collect customer sets.

## Setup

1. Set up the ring route (sushi shop and park spaces) and the betting board (2–12).
2. Each player's van starts at the sushi shop with 2 fixed action cards (Same Bet, One Extra Step); shuffle remaining action cards.
3. Reveal customer cards equal to the number of players.

## How to play

1. Bet: place your stone on an empty number (2–12); next round the lowest bidder bets first.
2. Challenge: the highest bidder rolls 2 dice up to twice and must reach at least their bet. Success: they and all lower bidders move. Failure: they take an action card as consolation and the next-highest tries.
3. Move by your bet, highest first. Reaching or passing the sushi shop takes 1 face-up customer card (max 1 per move); stopping exactly on the park takes an action card.
4. Refill: the lowest bidder decides whether leftover customers stay or are cleared; refill to player count.
5. Action cards: same bet, swap adjacent bets, reroll a die, one extra step, extra tip.

## Game end & scoring

- Ends when customer cards cannot be refilled.
- Each customer colour: first card 3 points, each additional 1 point (rare colours have only 2 cards).
- The player with the fewest tip cards loses points (amount per rulebook); highest total wins.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** Ends when customer cards cannot be refilled.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Bet: place your stone on an empty number (2–12); next round the lowest bidder bets first.
3. **Setup check:** Set up the ring route (sushi shop and park spaces) and the betting board (2–12).
4. **The one rule people forget:** Only the highest bidder rolls; lower bidders move if they succeed.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- High bets move far but risk failure.
- Rare colours are worth chasing.
- Do not ignore tips.

## Common mistakes

- Only the highest bidder rolls; lower bidders move if they succeed.
- At most one customer card per move.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** most board gamers
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

5. Game ends when customer cards run out.

  A: No movement; take an action card.

# Top Ten

> Chinese name: **脑洞量表 Top Ten** · Doc #92 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../92_%E8%84%91%E6%B4%9E%E9%87%8F%E8%A1%A8_Top_Ten.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/92)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 4–9 (best: 5-7) |
| Play time | 20–30 min |
| Age | 14+ |
| Complexity | ●○○○○ Very light (1/5) |
| Tags | co-op, party, word, association |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** D3 / E2 / E3 / E4 / E5 / F3 / G3
- **Photos:** [155102](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155102.jpg), [155106](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155106.jpg), [155109](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155109.jpg), [155125](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155125.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 第三轮照片 155102 / 155106：D3 层与 E3 层 → 脑洞量表 Top Ten
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155109：E2–E5 层与 D3 层 → ★ 我是大老板、★ 逃离亚特兰蒂斯、★ 狼人真言、★ 终极狼人、★ 脑洞量表、★ 纸牌帮 ×3、★ 骷髅与玫瑰 ×2、★ 星际探险队：深海任务、★ 大喜临门 ×2（与第三轮 D3 / E4 同一批）
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155125：F3 / G3 层（与第四轮重叠） → ★ 江湖郎中、★ 猜心俱乐部、★ 七大奇迹、★ 季节传说、★ 寿司狗（Gamewright 红色竹帘盒）、★ 奶酪大盗 ×2+、★ 女巫镇 ×4、★ 无间风云 ×3、★ 脑洞量表（青绿色盒）

## Overview

Fully cooperative: each player has a secret number 1–10 and answers a theme to express it; the captain orders the answers.

## Setup

1. Draw theme cards; set unicorn tokens (lives).

## How to play

1. The captain reads a theme; each player gives an answer reflecting their number.
2. The captain orders players from low to high; mistakes cost unicorn tokens.

## Game end & scoring

- Win by completing the theme deck before lives run out.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** Win by completing the theme deck before lives run out.
2. **What you do on a turn:** The captain reads a theme; each player gives an answer reflecting their number.
3. **Setup check:** Draw theme cards; set unicorn tokens (lives).
4. **The one rule people forget:** Never say numbers.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Spread answers across the range.
- Exaggerate for 1s and 10s.
- Red themes are adult-oriented.

## Common mistakes

- Never say numbers.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** big parties; friends who prefer working together; icebreakers; newcomers and grandparents can learn it instantly
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

# The Gang

> Chinese name: **纸牌帮 The Gang** · Doc #95 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../95_%E7%BA%B8%E7%89%8C%E5%B8%AE_The_Gang.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/95)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 3–6 (best: 4-5) |
| Play time | 20 min |
| Age | 10+ |
| Complexity | ●●○○○ Light (2/5) |
| Tags | co-op, poker, deduction |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** D3 / E2 / E3 / E4 / E5
- **Photos:** [155102](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155102.jpg), [155106](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155106.jpg), [155109](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155109.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 第三轮照片 155102 / 155106：D3 层与 E3 层 → 纸牌帮 The Gang
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155109：E2–E5 层与 D3 层 → ★ 我是大老板、★ 逃离亚特兰蒂斯、★ 狼人真言、★ 终极狼人、★ 脑洞量表、★ 纸牌帮 ×3、★ 骷髅与玫瑰 ×2、★ 星际探险队：深海任务、★ 大喜临门 ×2（与第三轮 D3 / E4 同一批）

## Overview

Cooperative Texas Hold'em: no talking about cards — take chips to signal your hand strength; the team must rank correctly.

## Setup

1. Deal 2 hole cards; four chip colours numbered 1 to players.

## How to play

1. Each betting round (pre-flop, flop, turn, river) take a chip of that colour expressing your rank; you may steal others' chips.
2. At showdown, check whether ranks match chip order.

## Game end & scoring

- 3 successful heists win; 3 failures lose.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** 3 successful heists win; 3 failures lose.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Each betting round (pre-flop, flop, turn, river) take a chip of that colour expressing your rank; you may steal others' chips.
3. **Setup check:** Deal 2 hole cards; four chip colours numbered 1 to players.
4. **The one rule people forget:** No verbal or facial signals.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Taking a chip is communication.
- Read community cards.
- Start cautious, refine later.

## Common mistakes

- No verbal or facial signals.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** friends who prefer working together; people who love deduction and guessing identities; short gaps — waiting for friends, before or after dinner
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

## Detailed supplement: components, worked example, scoring, FAQ, player counts, variants

> Written by the editor from the rules above as a walkthrough; numbers in the example are illustrative. The rulebook in the box always wins.

### Component checklist

From the Chinese doc (original wording):

- [ ] 52 张扑克牌、4 色筹码（白、黄、橙、红，每色 1 到人数号；颜色对应回合）、3 张金库卡、3 张警报卡、挑战卡与专家卡（进阶）。

Before you start:
- Count the components against the rulebook list; sort cards by type — a missing card can break the balance.
- Check tokens, dice and score markers; coins or generic tokens can stand in for missing pieces.
- Hand out reference cards before the first game to save rulebook time.

### Worked example (illustrative)

1. Cooperative: Texas Hold'em hands without talking about cards.
2. After 2 cards, take star chips 1–N for your perceived strength.
3. Repeat after flop, turn, river.
4. Reveal: hands must be in chip order.
5. Correct order cracks a vault.

### Scoring example

- 3 successes before 3 failures.

### Rules FAQ / rulings

- **Q: Can I say I'm strong?**  
  A: No, only via chips.

### Player-count differences

- 3–6.
- Best with: 4-5 players.

### Official variants & expansions

- Challenge and specialist cards.

## Advanced guide: teaching script, time budget, phase strategy, extra FAQ

> Summarised by the editor from the rules above for teaching and improving; the rulebook wins on conflicts.

### First-game time budget

- Teaching: about 10 min (weight ●●).
- Play: 20–20 min; budget about 25 min for a first game.
- Total: about 35 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> Co-op Texas Hold'em. No talking about cards — take star chips to express your hand's strength. Re-take chips after each community reveal. At showdown, if rankings match, you crack a vault. 3 vaults win; 3 alarms lose.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Take chips based on hole cards first.
- **Mid:** Watch others' hesitation.
- **Late:** Swap chips carefully at the end.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: Can I take someone's chip?**  
  A: Yes.
- **Q: Can I say my hand is good?**  
  A: No.

# Skull

> Chinese name: **骷髅与玫瑰 Skull** · Doc #96 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../96_%E9%AA%B7%E9%AB%85%E4%B8%8E%E7%8E%AB%E7%91%B0_Skull.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/96)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 3–6 (best: 4-6) |
| Play time | 15–45 min |
| Age | 10+ |
| Complexity | ●○○○○ Very light (1/5) |
| Tags | bluffing, push-your-luck, party, classic |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** D3 / E2 / E3 / E4 / E5
- **Photos:** [155102](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155102.jpg), [155106](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155106.jpg), [155109](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155109.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 第三轮照片 155102 / 155106：D3 层与 E3 层 → 骷髅与玫瑰 Skull（骷髅花纹盒 + 蓝色“Un jeu de Hervé Marly”盒）
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155109：E2–E5 层与 D3 层 → ★ 我是大老板、★ 逃离亚特兰蒂斯、★ 狼人真言、★ 终极狼人、★ 脑洞量表、★ 纸牌帮 ×3、★ 骷髅与玫瑰 ×2、★ 星际探险队：深海任务、★ 大喜临门 ×2（与第三轮 D3 / E4 同一批）

## Overview

Three roses and one skull each: place discs face down, then bid how many you can flip without hitting a skull.

## Setup

1. Each player has a mat and 4 discs (3 roses, 1 skull).

## How to play

1. Place a disc face down; continue placing or start a bid.
2. Bids rise; the highest bidder flips their own discs first, then others'.
3. Success: flip your mat (2 = win). Skull: lose a random disc.

## Game end & scoring

- Two successful challenges or last player with discs wins.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** Two successful challenges or last player with discs wins.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Place a disc face down; continue placing or start a bid.
3. **Setup check:** Each player has a mat and 4 discs (3 roses, 1 skull).
4. **The one rule people forget:** The skull's owner chooses which of your discs you lose.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Place your skull then bid high to scare.
- You flip your own first — careful.
- Watch escalating bidders.

## Common mistakes

- The skull's owner chooses which of your discs you lose.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** talkative groups who enjoy acting and bluffing; icebreakers; newcomers and grandparents can learn it instantly
- **Not ideal for:** players who dislike conflict or backstabbing

## Detailed supplement: components, worked example, scoring, FAQ, player counts, variants

> Written by the editor from the rules above as a walkthrough; numbers in the example are illustrative. The rulebook in the box always wins.

### Component checklist

From the Chinese doc (original wording):

- [ ] 每人 1 块杯垫（翻面表示赢过 1 次）+ 4 张圆片：**3 张玫瑰、1 张骷髅**。

Before you start:
- Count the components against the rulebook list; sort cards by type — a missing card can break the balance.
- Check tokens, dice and score markers; coins or generic tokens can stand in for missing pieces.
- Hand out reference cards before the first game to save rulebook time.

### Worked example (illustrative)

1. 4 coasters: 3 roses, 1 skull.
2. Everyone places a coaster face down.
3. I bid: 'I can flip 3 without a skull.'
4. Others raise or pass; I must flip mine first.
5. I hit a skull and lose a coaster.

### Scoring example

- Two successes, or last with coasters.

### Rules FAQ / rulings

- **Q: Must I flip mine first?**  
  A: Yes.

### Player-count differences

- 3–6.
- Best with: 4-6 players.

### Official variants & expansions

- See rulebook.

## Advanced guide: teaching script, time budget, phase strategy, extra FAQ

> Summarised by the editor from the rules above for teaching and improving; the rulebook wins on conflicts.

### First-game time budget

- Teaching: about 5 min (weight ●).
- Play: 15–45 min; budget about 55 min for a first game.
- Total: about 60 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> Three roses and a skull each. Place discs face down or bid how many you can flip without a skull. The challenger flips all their own first, then others'. Two successes win; failure loses a disc.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Read others' placing habits.
- **Mid:** Plant skulls to bait.
- **Late:** Bid considering your own discs.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: Must I flip my own first?**  
  A: Yes.
- **Q: Hit my own skull?**  
  A: I choose which disc to lose.

# The Crew: Mission Deep Sea

> Chinese name: **星际探险队：深海** · Doc #97 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../97_%E6%98%9F%E9%99%85%E6%8E%A2%E9%99%A9%E9%98%9F%E6%B7%B1%E6%B5%B7_The_Crew_Mission_Deep_Sea.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/97)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 2–5 (best: 4) |
| Play time | 20 min |
| Age | 10+ |
| Complexity | ●●○○○ Light (2/5) |
| Tags | co-op, trick-taking, campaign, cards |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** D3 / E2 / E3 / E4 / E5
- **Photos:** [155102](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155102.jpg), [155106](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155106.jpg), [155109](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155109.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 第三轮照片 155102 / 155106：D3 层与 E3 层 → 星际探险队：深海任务 The Crew: Mission Deep Sea
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155109：E2–E5 层与 D3 层 → ★ 我是大老板、★ 逃离亚特兰蒂斯、★ 狼人真言、★ 终极狼人、★ 脑洞量表、★ 纸牌帮 ×3、★ 骷髅与玫瑰 ×2、★ 星际探险队：深海任务、★ 大喜临门 ×2（与第三轮 D3 / E4 同一批）

## Overview

Cooperative trick-taking with task cards and limited communication across many missions.

## Setup

1. Deal all cards; the submarine 4 holder is captain.
2. Draw tasks to reach the mission's difficulty.

## How to play

1. Follow suit; submarines are trumps.
2. Each player may communicate once: show a card and mark it highest, lowest or only.

## Game end & scoring

- Complete all tasks to succeed and advance.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** Complete all tasks to succeed and advance.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Follow suit; submarines are trumps.
3. **Setup check:** Deal all cards; the submarine 4 holder is captain.
4. **The one rule people forget:** No discussion of hands.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Use communication on key suits.
- Hold trumps for necessary tricks.
- Complete hardest tasks first.

## Common mistakes

- No discussion of hands.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** friends who prefer working together; short gaps — waiting for friends, before or after dinner
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

## Detailed supplement: components, worked example, scoring, FAQ, player counts, variants

Before you start:
- Count the components against the rulebook list; sort cards by type — a missing card can break the balance.
- Check tokens, dice and score markers; coins or generic tokens can stand in for missing pieces.
- Hand out reference cards before the first game to save rulebook time.

### Worked example (illustrative)

1. Each mission sets task conditions.
2. No talking about cards.
3. I use a communication token to reveal one card.
4. We play tricks and I win the required one.
5. Mission done.

### Scoring example

- Cooperative; record attempts.

### Rules FAQ / rulings

- **Q: Can I say I have no yellow?**  
  A: No.

### Player-count differences

- 2–5.
- Best with: 4 players.

### Official variants & expansions

- Difficulty-rated missions.

## Advanced guide: teaching script, time budget, phase strategy, extra FAQ

> Summarised by the editor from the rules above for teaching and improving; the rulebook wins on conflicts.

### First-game time budget

- Teaching: about 10 min (weight ●●).
- Play: 20–20 min; budget about 25 min for a first game.
- Total: about 35 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> Co-op trick-taking. Complete task cards like 'win all pink tricks'. Follow suit; submarines are trump. Communication is limited — once per mission show a card with a token meaning highest/only/lowest of that colour.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Pick tasks that fit your hand.
- **Mid:** Save communication for key cards.
- **Late:** Control every trick late.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: Can I say I have no yellow?**  
  A: No.
- **Q: Difficulty in Deep Sea?**  
  A: By mission difficulty values.

# Werewords

> Chinese name: **狼人真言 Werewords** · Doc #98 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../98_%E7%8B%BC%E4%BA%BA%E7%9C%9F%E8%A8%80_Werewords.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/98)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 4–10 (best: 6-8) |
| Play time | 10 min |
| Age | 8+ |
| Complexity | ●○○○○ Very light (1/5) |
| Tags | word guessing, hidden role, party, deduction |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** D3 / E2 / E3 / E4 / E5
- **Photos:** [155102](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155102.jpg), [155106](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155106.jpg), [155109](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155109.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 第三轮照片 155102 / 155106：D3 层与 E3 层 → 狼人真言 Werewords
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155109：E2–E5 层与 D3 层 → ★ 我是大老板、★ 逃离亚特兰蒂斯、★ 狼人真言、★ 终极狼人、★ 脑洞量表、★ 纸牌帮 ×3、★ 骷髅与玫瑰 ×2、★ 星际探险队：深海任务、★ 大喜临门 ×2（与第三轮 D3 / E4 同一批）

## Overview

20 questions plus werewolves: the mayor answers yes/no; werewolves know the word and mislead; the seer must stay hidden.

## Setup

1. Use the app; deal roles; the mayor picks a word; werewolf and seer learn it.

## How to play

1. Players ask yes/no questions; the mayor answers with tokens.
2. Guess within time.

## Game end & scoring

- Word guessed: werewolves try to find the seer.
- Not guessed: everyone votes for a werewolf.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** Word guessed: werewolves try to find the seer.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Players ask yes/no questions; the mayor answers with tokens.
3. **Setup check:** Use the app; deal roles; the mayor picks a word; werewolf and seer learn it.
4. **The one rule people forget:** The mayor communicates only with tokens.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Seer guides subtly.
- Werewolves waste time with good-sounding questions.
- 'So close' tokens are information.

## Common mistakes

- The mayor communicates only with tokens.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** big parties; people who love deduction and guessing identities; icebreakers; newcomers and grandparents can learn it instantly; short gaps — waiting for friends, before or after dinner
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

## Detailed supplement: components, worked example, scoring, FAQ, player counts, variants

> Written by the editor from the rules above as a walkthrough; numbers in the example are illustrative. The rulebook in the box always wins.

### Component checklist

- [ ] No complete public component list — use the list in the box.

Before you start:
- Count the components against the rulebook list; sort cards by type — a missing card can break the balance.
- Check tokens, dice and score markers; coins or generic tokens can stand in for missing pieces.
- Hand out reference cards before the first game to save rulebook time.

### Worked example (illustrative)

1. The mayor picks a secret word.
2. Werewolves and the Seer know it.
3. Players ask yes/no questions.
4. Villagers guess it in time; the werewolves try to find the Seer.
5. Werewolves find the Seer and win.

### Scoring example

- Guessed: wolves win by finding the Seer; not guessed: villagers win by finding a wolf.

### Rules FAQ / rulings

- **Q: Can the mayor be a wolf?**  
  A: Yes.

### Player-count differences

- 4–10.
- Best with: 6-8 players.

### Official variants & expansions

- Uses an app.

## Advanced guide: teaching script, time budget, phase strategy, extra FAQ

> Summarised by the editor from the rules above for teaching and improving; the rulebook wins on conflicts.

### First-game time budget

- Teaching: about 5 min (weight ●).
- Play: 10–10 min; budget about 10 min for a first game.
- Total: about 15 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> Twenty questions plus werewolves. The mayor picks a word and answers yes/no. Werewolves know it and mislead; the seer knows and guides. If guessed, werewolves try to find the seer; if not, villagers try to find a werewolf.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Ask category questions first.
- **Mid:** Watch for too-accurate or misleading questions.
- **Late:** Seer stay low-key.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: Can the mayor be a werewolf?**  
  A: Yes.
- **Q: Need the app?**  
  A: For timing; substitutes exist.

# Ultimate Werewolf

> Chinese name: **终极狼人** · Doc #99 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../99_%E7%BB%88%E6%9E%81%E7%8B%BC%E4%BA%BA_Ultimate_Werewolf.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/99)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 5–75 (best: 8-15) |
| Play time | 30–90 min |
| Age | 9+ |
| Complexity | ●●○○○ Light (2/5) |
| Tags | teams, deduction, hidden role, elimination, party, classic |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** D3 / E2 / E3 / E4 / E5
- **Photos:** [155102](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155102.jpg), [155106](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155106.jpg), [155109](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155109.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 第三轮照片 155102 / 155106：D3 层与 E3 层 → 终极狼人 Ultimate Werewolf
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155109：E2–E5 层与 D3 层 → ★ 我是大老板、★ 逃离亚特兰蒂斯、★ 狼人真言、★ 终极狼人、★ 脑洞量表、★ 纸牌帮 ×3、★ 骷髅与玫瑰 ×2、★ 星际探险队：深海任务、★ 大喜临门 ×2（与第三轮 D3 / E4 同一批）

## Overview

Classic werewolf: night kills and powers, day discussion and votes; villagers must eliminate all wolves.

## Setup

1. A moderator distributes balanced roles.

## How to play

1. Night: werewolves kill, special roles act.
2. Day: discuss and vote to eliminate.

## Game end & scoring

- Villagers win when all wolves are gone; wolves win at parity.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** Villagers win when all wolves are gone; wolves win at parity.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Night: werewolves kill, special roles act.
3. **Setup check:** A moderator distributes balanced roles.
4. **The one rule people forget:** Eliminated players stay silent.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Keep logical consistency.
- Seers time reveals.
- Wolves split their votes.

## Common mistakes

- Eliminated players stay silent.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** big parties; people who love deduction and guessing identities
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

## Detailed supplement: components, worked example, scoring, FAQ, player counts, variants

> Written by the editor from the rules above as a walkthrough; numbers in the example are illustrative. The rulebook in the box always wins.

### Component checklist

- [ ] No complete public component list — use the list in the box.

Before you start:
- Count the components against the rulebook list; sort cards by type — a missing card can break the balance.
- Check tokens, dice and score markers; coins or generic tokens can stand in for missing pieces.
- Hand out reference cards before the first game to save rulebook time.

### Worked example (illustrative)

1. The moderator deals roles; night: werewolves kill.
2. The seer checks someone; other roles act.
3. Day: discuss and vote to eliminate.
4. Reveal the eliminated role.
5. All wolves dead: village wins; wolves equal villagers: wolves win.

### Scoring example

- Win per team conditions.

### Rules FAQ / rulings

- **Q: Can the dead speak?**  
  A: Usually not.

### Player-count differences

- Check the box; best with 5+.
- Best with: 8-15 players.

### Official variants & expansions

- Many roles.

## Advanced guide: teaching script, time budget, phase strategy, extra FAQ

> Summarised by the editor from the rules above for teaching and improving; the rulebook wins on conflicts.

### First-game time budget

- Teaching: about 10 min (weight ●●).
- Play: 30–90 min; budget about 110 min for a first game.
- Total: about 120 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> Classic werewolf. At night wolves kill and specials act (seer checks, witch potions, guard protects). By day discuss and exile. Good wins by eliminating wolves; wolves win by parity.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Good: find logic holes.
- **Mid:** Wolves: fake claims and confuse.
- **Late:** Coordinate key votes.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: Can the dead speak?**  
  A: Last words usually.
- **Q: Tied vote?**  
  A: Tiebreak or no exile.

<a id="g100"></a>

# I'm the Boss!

> Chinese name: **我是大老板 I'm the Boss** · Doc #100 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../100_%E6%88%91%E6%98%AF%E5%A4%A7%E8%80%81%E6%9D%BF_Im_the_Boss.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/100)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 3–6 (best: 5-6) |
| Play time | 60 min |
| Age | 12+ |
| Complexity | ●●○○○ Light (2/5) |
| Tags | negotiation, talky, trading, classic |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** D3 / E2 / E4 / E5 / F5
- **Photos:** [155102](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155102.jpg), [155109](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155109.jpg), [155128](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155128.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 第三轮照片 155102：E4 层 → 我是大老板 I'm the Boss!
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155109：E2–E5 层与 D3 层 → ★ 我是大老板、★ 逃离亚特兰蒂斯、★ 狼人真言、★ 终极狼人、★ 脑洞量表、★ 纸牌帮 ×3、★ 骷髅与玫瑰 ×2、★ 星际探险队：深海任务、★ 大喜临门 ×2（与第三轮 D3 / E4 同一批）
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155128：F5 下方与左侧 → ★ 签签入扣 Stick Stack ×2、★ 我是大老板（左上）、★ 香料世纪（左侧 E 列）

## Overview

Sid Sackson's pure negotiation classic: deals need specific investors; the boss haggles the split, and influence cards send investors travelling or seize the deal.

## Setup

1. Deal the 6 investor cards face up (extras aside in small games); deal 5 influence cards each.
2. Stack deal cards by number; the first player is earliest alphabetically by investor; the player on their right places the dollar marker on any space.

## How to play

1. On your turn: negotiate the deal where the dollar marker is, OR roll to move the marker clockwise (counting only unclosed deals) then negotiate or draw 3 influence cards.
2. The initiator is the boss and must assemble all required investors.
3. Profit = dividends on the space × current share price; the boss and participants negotiate the split — no advance payments.
4. During negotiation anyone may play influence cards (send investors travelling, take over as boss, etc.).
5. When all investors are in, the boss announces terms and says 'going once, twice — deal!'; no more influence cards, the bank pays.
6. If required investors are all travelling and nobody can substitute, the deal fails.

## Game end & scoring

- After the final deal card, the richest player wins.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** After the final deal card, the richest player wins.
2. **What you do on a turn:** On your turn: negotiate the deal where the dollar marker is, OR roll to move the marker clockwise (counting only unclosed deals) then negotiate or draw 3 influence cards.
3. **Setup check:** Deal the 6 investor cards face up (extras aside in small games); deal 5 influence cards each.
4. **The one rule people forget:** Promises are non-binding and nothing is paid in advance.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Scarce investors give leverage.
- Play 'I'm the Boss' after others agree.
- Credibility is long-term capital.

## Common mistakes

- Promises are non-binding and nothing is paid in advance.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** talkative groups who enjoy acting and bluffing
- **Not ideal for:** players who dislike conflict or backstabbing

## Detailed supplement: components, worked example, scoring, FAQ, player counts, variants

> Written by the editor from the rules above as a walkthrough; numbers in the example are illustrative. The rulebook in the box always wins.

### Component checklist

From the Chinese doc (original wording):

- [ ] 游戏板（一圈“生意”格，每格写着需要哪些**投资者家族**以及分红数）、15 张交易卡（股价）、6 张**投资者牌**（两面同图）、98 张**影响力卡**、美元标记、钞票。

Before you start:
- Count the components against the rulebook list; sort cards by type — a missing card can break the balance.
- Check tokens, dice and score markers; coins or generic tokens can stand in for missing pieces.
- Hand out reference cards before the first game to save rulebook time.

### Worked example (illustrative)

1. The dollar marker stops on a deal; I become boss.
2. It needs 3 families; I own 1 and must buy the others.
3. A asks 40%; B plays a travel card.
4. I play a family card to replace B.
5. Profit = dividend × share price, split as agreed.

### Scoring example

- Most money when the deal cards run out.

### Rules FAQ / rulings

- **Q: Are promises binding?**  
  A: No.

### Player-count differences

- 3–6.
- Best with: 5-6 players.

### Official variants & expansions

- See rulebook.

## Advanced guide: teaching script, time budget, phase strategy, extra FAQ

> Summarised by the editor from the rules above for teaching and improving; the rulebook wins on conflicts.

### First-game time budget

- Teaching: about 10 min (weight ●●).
- Play: 60–60 min; budget about 70 min for a first game.
- Total: about 80 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> Pure negotiation. Each deal needs certain investor families; haggle over shares and disrupt with cards — send families on holiday, swap them. Most money wins.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Collect families and cards.
- **Mid:** Control key families.
- **Late:** Max shares in the last deals.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: Are deals binding?**  
  A: Agreed shares must be paid.
- **Q: Can the boss pass?**  
  A: Yes, no profit.

# Survive: Escape from Atlantis!

> Chinese name: **逃离亚特兰蒂斯** · Doc #101 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../101_%E9%80%83%E7%A6%BB%E4%BA%9A%E7%89%B9%E5%85%B0%E8%92%82%E6%96%AF_Survive_Escape_from_Atlantis.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/101)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 2–4 (best: 4) |
| Play time | 45–60 min |
| Age | 8+ |
| Complexity | ●●○○○ Light (2/5) |
| Tags | family, take-that, movement, classic |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** D3 / E2 / E4 / E5
- **Photos:** [155102](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155102.jpg), [155109](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155109.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 第三轮照片 155102：E4 层 → 逃离亚特兰蒂斯 Survive: Escape from Atlantis!
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155109：E2–E5 层与 D3 层 → ★ 我是大老板、★ 逃离亚特兰蒂斯、★ 狼人真言、★ 终极狼人、★ 脑洞量表、★ 纸牌帮 ×3、★ 骷髅与玫瑰 ×2、★ 星际探险队：深海任务、★ 大喜临门 ×2（与第三轮 D3 / E4 同一批）

## Overview

The island sinks; rescue your people to safety while sharks, sea serpents and whales hunt others.

## Setup

1. Build the island (beach, forest, mountain); place people (hidden values) and boats.

## How to play

1. Play a held tile; move 3 spaces; remove a land tile (beach → forest → mountain); roll the creature die and move one.

## Game end & scoring

- When the volcano appears, the game ends; score rescued people's hidden values.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** When the volcano appears, the game ends; score rescued people's hidden values.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Play a held tile; move 3 spaces; remove a land tile (beach → forest → mountain); roll the creature die and move one.
3. **Setup check:** Build the island (beach, forest, mountain); place people (hidden values) and boats.
4. **The one rule people forget:** Remove tiles outside-in.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Evacuate high-value people first.
- Removed tiles may give immediate or held effects.
- Target full boats.

## Common mistakes

- Remove tiles outside-in.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** most board gamers
- **Not ideal for:** players who dislike conflict or backstabbing

## Detailed supplement: components, worked example, scoring, FAQ, player counts, variants

> Written by the editor from the rules above as a walkthrough; numbers in the example are illustrative. The rulebook in the box always wins.

### Component checklist

From the Chinese doc (original wording):

- [ ] 海洋版图、40 块岛屿板块（沙滩、森林、山地三层，背面有事件）、每人 10 个探险家（底部印 1–6 分，自己可以偷看，他人看不到）、小船、鲨鱼、鲸鱼、海蛇、生物骰。

Before you start:
- Count the components against the rulebook list; sort cards by type — a missing card can break the balance.
- Check tokens, dice and score markers; coins or generic tokens can stand in for missing pieces.
- Hand out reference cards before the first game to save rulebook time.

### Worked example (illustrative)

1. Place people on the island.
2. Move up to 3 points.
3. Remove a tile: a shark appears.
4. Roll the creature die: a whale capsizes a boat.
5. Volcano erupts; score rescued people.

### Scoring example

- Sum rescued people's values.

### Rules FAQ / rulings

- See “Common mistakes” above; anything else — check the rulebook.

### Player-count differences

- 2–4.
- Best with: 4 players.

### Official variants & expansions

- 5–6 player expansion.

## Advanced guide: teaching script, time budget, phase strategy, extra FAQ

> Summarised by the editor from the rules above for teaching and improving; the rulebook wins on conflicts.

### First-game time budget

- Teaching: about 10 min (weight ●●).
- Play: 45–60 min; budget about 70 min for a first game.
- Total: about 80 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> The island sinks — get your people to safety. Move people and boats, remove a tile (with effects), then roll to move sharks, sea serpents or whales to harass others. Score the values on rescued people.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Move high-value people toward the edge.
- **Mid:** Use boats for multiple people.
- **Late:** Send monsters at the leader.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: Can I see people values?**  
  A: Only your own.
- **Q: Can serpents sink boats?**  
  A: Yes.
