## Overview

The harbour master auction: place workers on boats, ports, shipyards, pirates or navigators; roll dice to move boats.

## Setup

1. Auction harbour master; distribute shares.

## How to play

1. Harbour master chooses cargo and starting positions.
2. Place workers in turn.
3. Roll dice three times to move boats; resolve payouts.

## Game end & scoring

- When a cargo price reaches 30, the game ends; richest wins.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** When a cargo price reaches 30, the game ends; richest wins.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Harbour master chooses cargo and starting positions.
3. **Setup check:** Auction harbour master; distribute shares.
4. **The one rule people forget:** Harbour master has privileges.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Load high-value cargo.
- Navigators decide landings.
- Pirates raid at space 13.

## Common mistakes

- Harbour master has privileges.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** most board gamers
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155125 / 155128：F4 / G4 层 → ★ 骆驼快跑 ×2、★ 四海好伙伴、★ 玩具大战（贴 A3 标签）、★ 犯罪现场（G4，第 2 盒）

## Overview

The forensic scientist knows the truth but can only use clue tiles; investigators deduce the means and key evidence; the murderer misleads.

## Setup

1. Deal roles (scientist, murderer, investigators; optional accomplice and witness); each investigator shows means and clue cards.

## How to play

1. The murderer secretly selects a means and evidence.
2. The scientist places bullets on scene tiles.
3. Each player has one badge to solve.

## Game end & scoring

- Correct solution: investigators win; otherwise murderer wins (witness rules optional).

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** Correct solution: investigators win; otherwise murderer wins (witness rules optional).
2. **What you do on a turn:** The murderer secretly selects a means and evidence.
3. **Setup check:** Deal roles (scientist, murderer, investigators; optional accomplice and witness); each investigator shows means and clue cards.
4. **The one rule people forget:** The scientist may not speak.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Scientist narrows with cause and location tiles first.
- Murderer steers attention.
- One guess only.

## Common mistakes

- The scientist may not speak.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** big parties; short gaps — waiting for friends, before or after dinner
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

## Overview

A realistic experience game: vote on choices in the life of Lee Ji-hye, an ordinary Korean woman; family and friends guard attributes.

## Setup

1. One player is Lee Ji-hye; others take guardian roles (father, mother, brother, friends).

## How to play

1. Read situations, vote on choices, adjust attributes.

## Game end & scoring

- Read the answer booklet and reflect together.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** Read the answer booklet and reflect together.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Read situations, vote on choices, adjust attributes.
3. **Setup check:** One player is Lee Ji-hye; others take guardian roles (father, mother, brother, friends).
4. **The one rule people forget:** Serious themes.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Discussion matters more than winning.
- Speak for your attribute.
- Reflect afterwards.

## Common mistakes

- Serious themes.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** friends who prefer working together
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

# Goose Telephone

> Chinese name: **以鹅传鹅** · Doc #137 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../137_%E4%BB%A5%E9%B9%85%E4%BC%A0%E9%B9%85.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/137)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 5–9 (best: 6-8) |
| Play time | 20–30 min |
| Age | 10+ |
| Complexity | ●○○○○ Very light (1/5) |
| Tags | party, charades, telephone, hidden role |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** F3 / G2 / G3 / G4
- **Photos:** [155121](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155121.jpg), [155125](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155125.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155121：G2–G4 层 → 以鹅传鹅
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155125：F3 / G3 层（与第四轮重叠） → ★ 犯罪现场（千骐 / 千智）、★ 李智慧生存游戏、★ 以鹅传鹅 ×2、★ 只言片语、★ 浮生青云录

## Overview

Charades telephone with roles: the head goose sees the case and passes it on with gestures and honks; a bad goose sabotages.

## Setup

1. Roles: head goose, bad goose, good geese.

## How to play

1. Head goose views the culprit and victim cards.
2. Pass by gestures and honks only.
3. Guess from the last player backwards; then vote for the bad goose.

## Game end & scoring

- Head and good geese win if the case is guessed and the bad goose is caught.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** Head and good geese win if the case is guessed and the bad goose is caught.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Head goose views the culprit and victim cards.
3. **Setup check:** Roles: head goose, bad goose, good geese.
4. **The one rule people forget:** No speaking.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Keep gestures simple.
- Bad goose distorts subtly.
- Remember each gesture.

## Common mistakes

- No speaking.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** big parties; people who love deduction and guessing identities; icebreakers; newcomers and grandparents can learn it instantly
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

# Floating Life Rising Clouds

> Chinese name: **浮生青云录** · Doc #138 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../138_%E6%B5%AE%E7%94%9F%E9%9D%92%E4%BA%91%E5%BD%95.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/138)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 2–6 (best: 3-4) |
| Play time | 30 min |
| Age | 8+ |
| Complexity | ●○○○○ Very light (1/5) |
| Tags | Chinese style, dice, movement, blocking, family |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** F3 / G2 / G3 / G4
- **Photos:** [155121](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155121.jpg), [155125](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155125.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155121：G2–G4 层 → 浮生青云录
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155125：F3 / G3 层（与第四轮重叠） → ★ 犯罪现场（千骐 / 千智）、★ 李智慧生存游戏、★ 以鹅传鹅 ×2、★ 只言片语、★ 浮生青云录

## Overview

Chinese-style light strategy (Yaofish Games): scholars rise and fall in officialdom; roll and move exactly, take tiles you leave empty, and use koi tiles to flip negatives.

## Setup

1. Components: 8 fortune tiles, 18 adversity tiles, start and end tiles, 32 floating-life tiles, 18 scholars, 8 guards, 1 die.
2. Lay out the path from start to end; place scholars and guards per rulebook.

## How to play

1. Roll one six-sided die.
2. Choose one, moving exactly the rolled number: move one of your scholars — if the tile you leave has no scholars or guards, you MUST take it as points (positive or negative); or move a guard that shares a tile with any scholar — no points, but it changes who empties tiles.

## Game end & scoring

- The game ends immediately when all scholars reach the end.
- Sum positive and negative tiles; each koi tile flips one negative tile to equal positive (e.g. −6 → +6).
- Highest total wins; variant rules are included.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** The game ends immediately when all scholars reach the end.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Roll one six-sided die.
3. **Setup check:** Components: 8 fortune tiles, 18 adversity tiles, start and end tiles, 32 floating-life tiles, 18 scholars, 8 guards, 1 die.
4. **The one rule people forget:** Movement must use the full roll.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Guards can force opponents to take bad tiles.
- Save koi for your biggest negative.
- Count exact distances.

## Common mistakes

- Movement must use the full roll.
- Taking an emptied tile is mandatory.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** icebreakers; newcomers and grandparents can learn it instantly
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

3. Leaving an empty tile triggers the rule.
4. Opponents move guards.
5. Score fortune and adversity tiles.

- 2–6.

### Strategy by phase

- **Mid:** Block with guards.
- **Late:** Plan exact moves late.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: Must I use the full roll?**  
  A: Yes.
- **Q: Can guards move?**  
  A: Yes.

# Good Critters

> Chinese name: **四海好伙伴 Good Critters** · Doc #139 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../139_%E5%9B%9B%E6%B5%B7%E5%A5%BD%E4%BC%99%E4%BC%B4_GoodCritters.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/139)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 4–8 (best: 5-7) |
| Play time | 30–45 min |
| Age | 12+ |
| Complexity | ●○○○○ Very light (1/5) |
| Tags | negotiation, loot split, voting, talky |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** A3 / F4 / G2 / G4
- **Photos:** [155121](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155121.jpg), [155125](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155125.jpg), [155128](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155128.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155121：G2–G4 层 → 四海好伙伴 GoodCritters
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155125 / 155128：F4 / G4 层 → ★ 骆驼快跑 ×2、★ 四海好伙伴、★ 玩具大战（贴 A3 标签）、★ 犯罪现场（G4，第 2 盒）

## Overview

Animal gang splitting loot: the boss divides, everyone secretly votes, sneaks, robs or guards; 'The Fuzz!' ends the game.

## Setup

1. Shuffle loot and put The Fuzz! randomly in the bottom half.
2. Each player takes action cards (Aye, Nay, Sneak, Guard, Rob) and threat tokens; the host is the first boss.

## How to play

1. Reveal players + 2 loot cards; the boss divides them freely (may exclude people) but must distribute all.
2. Everyone secretly plays 1 action card; resolve from the boss in seat order.
3. Sneak: take the top card of the deck (only the first sneak in order succeeds). Rob: take a random loot card from a player you threatened. Guard: anyone robbing you loses a card to you.
4. More ayes: the split stands and the boss stays. More nays: the split is void; the first nay voter clockwise from the boss becomes boss, 2 more cards are added and redivided.

## Game end & scoring

- When The Fuzz! is revealed, highest loot value wins.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** When The Fuzz! is revealed, highest loot value wins.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Reveal players + 2 loot cards; the boss divides them freely (may exclude people) but must distribute all.
3. **Setup check:** Shuffle loot and put The Fuzz! randomly in the bottom half.
4. **The one rule people forget:** Only the first sneak succeeds each round.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Give enough to pass votes.
- Threaten before robbing.
- Guard counters robbers.

## Common mistakes

- Only the first sneak succeeds each round.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** big parties; talkative groups who enjoy acting and bluffing; icebreakers; newcomers and grandparents can learn it instantly
- **Not ideal for:** players who dislike conflict or backstabbing

- Hand out reference cards before the first game to save rulebook time.

### Worked example (illustrative)

1. The boss reveals players + 2 loot cards and splits them.
2. Everyone secretly plays an action card.
3. Resolve from the boss: more nays than ayes cancels the split.
4. The first nay voter clockwise becomes boss.
5. When 'The Fuzz!' appears, highest loot wins.

### Scoring example

- Highest loot value.

### Rules FAQ / rulings

- **Q: Tie vote?**  
  A: Split stands; boss passes on.

### Player-count differences

- 4–8.
- Best with: 5-7 players.

### Official variants & expansions

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Read the boss's splits.
- **Mid:** Time your robberies.
- **Late:** Protect your lead.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: Reject the split?**  
  A: Vote no.
- **Q: When do cops arrive?**  
  A: Per rulebook.

# The Resistance: Avalon

> Chinese name: **阿瓦隆 Avalon** · Doc #140 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../140_%E9%98%BF%E7%93%A6%E9%9A%86_The_Resistance_Avalon.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/140)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 5–10 (best: 7-8) |
| Play time | 30 min |
| Age | 13+ |
| Complexity | ●●○○○ Light (2/5) |
| Tags | teams, deduction, hidden role, no elimination, classic |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** D3 / E2 / E5
- **Photos:** [155109](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155109.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155109：E2–E5 层与 D3 层 → 阿瓦隆 The Resistance: Avalon（紫色盒，⚠️ 只露出“阿瓦…”）

## Overview

Loyal servants of Arthur vs minions of Mordred: leaders propose teams, everyone votes, quests succeed or fail; Merlin must stay hidden.

## Setup

1. Roles by player count; Merlin and evil see appropriate info.

## How to play

1. Leader proposes a team; everyone votes; 5 rejections = evil wins.
2. Team members secretly play success/fail.

## Game end & scoring

- 3 successes then the assassin guesses Merlin; 3 fails = evil wins.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** 3 successes then the assassin guesses Merlin; 3 fails = evil wins.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Leader proposes a team; everyone votes; 5 rejections = evil wins.
3. **Setup check:** Roles by player count; Merlin and evil see appropriate info.
4. **The one rule people forget:** Quest 4 needs two fails with 7+ players.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Merlin hints subtly.
- Evil sometimes passes.
- Percival protects Merlin.

## Common mistakes

- Quest 4 needs two fails with 7+ players.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** big parties; people who love deduction and guessing identities
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

3. Team plays success/fail.
4. Good completes 3 quests.

- 3 successes good; 3 fails evil.

  A: Evil wins.

### First-game time budget

- Teaching: about 10 min (weight ●●).
- Play: 30–30 min; budget about 35 min for a first game.
- Total: about 45 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> Loyal knights versus Mordred's minions. The leader proposes a team; everyone votes. Approved team members secretly play success or fail — one fail sinks a quest (two on quest 4 with 7+). Three successes win for good unless the Assassin finds Merlin; three fails win for evil.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Good: track vote history.
- **Mid:** Merlin: hint subtly.
- **Late:** Evil: hunt Merlin at the end.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: Five rejected votes?**  
  A: Evil wins.
- **Q: What does Percival see?**  
  A: Merlin and Morgana, indistinguishable.

# Ubongo

> Chinese name: **乌邦果 Ubongo** · Doc #146 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../146_%E4%B9%8C%E9%82%A6%E6%9E%9C_Ubongo.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/146)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 1–4 (best: 2-4) |
| Play time | 25–30 min |
| Age | 8+ |
| Complexity | ●●○○○ Light (2/5) |
| Tags | real-time, spatial, puzzle, race, family |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** F2 / F3
- **Photos:** [155114](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155114.jpg), [155121](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155121.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155114 / 155121：F2 / F3 行 → 乌邦果 Ubongo（基础版，KOSMOS 红盒，⚠️ 标题没拍全）

## Overview

Real-time puzzle race: roll to see which pieces to use and fill your board before the timer runs out.

## Setup

1. Each player has 12 pieces and puzzle cards (3- and 4-piece sides).

## How to play

1. Roll the die; check which pieces; flip the timer.
2. Fill the board exactly; call Ubongo; take gems in order.

## Game end & scoring

- After 9 rounds, most gems of one colour wins (per rulebook).

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** After 9 rounds, most gems of one colour wins (per rulebook).
2. **What you do on a turn:** Roll the die; check which pieces; flip the timer.
3. **Setup check:** Each player has 12 pieces and puzzle cards (3- and 4-piece sides).
4. **The one rule people forget:** Exact fill only.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Largest piece first.
- Pieces may be flipped.
- Pick gem colours wisely.

## Common mistakes

- Exact fill only.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** fans of speed and excitement
- **Not ideal for:** people who want to think slowly and quietly

## Detailed supplement: components, worked example, scoring, FAQ, player counts, variants

- [ ] 计时沙漏
- [ ] 符号骰
- [ ] 宝石版图

- Count the components against the rulebook list; sort cards by type — a missing card can break the balance.
- Check tokens, dice and score markers; coins or generic tokens can stand in for missing pieces.
- Hand out reference cards before the first game to save rulebook time.

### Worked example (illustrative)

1. Same pieces and puzzle cards.
2. Roll to choose pieces.
3. Race to fill the shape.
4. First shouts Ubongo.
5. Most gems wins.

### Scoring example

- After 9 rounds, most gems of a single colour wins; ties by total gems (see rulebook).

### Rules FAQ / rulings

- See “Common mistakes” above; anything else — check the rulebook.

### Player-count differences

- 1–4.
- Best with: 2-4 players.

### Official variants & expansions

- Easy/hard sides.

## Advanced guide: teaching script, time budget, phase strategy, extra FAQ

> Summarised by the editor from the rules above for teaching and improving; the rulebook wins on conflicts.

### First-game time budget

- Teaching: about 10 min (weight ●●).
- Play: 25–30 min; budget about 35 min for a first game.
- Total: about 45 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> Each player gets a puzzle card. Roll the symbol die to choose the row of 3 (or 4) pieces; flip the timer. Everyone fills the blank area exactly. Shout 'Ubongo!' to claim reward gems in order; anyone done in time takes gems. After 9 rounds the player with the most gems of one colour wins; ties by total gems.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Warm up on the 3-piece side.
- **Mid:** Place the biggest piece first.
- **Late:** Focus on one gem colour.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: Can pieces overhang?**  
  A: No.
- **Q: How is the winner decided?**  
  A: Most of one colour; ties by total.

# The Message (Fengsheng)

> Chinese name: **风声 The Message** · Doc #147 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../147_%E9%A3%8E%E5%A3%B0_The_Message.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/147)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 3–9 (best: 6-8) |
| Play time | 30–60 min |
| Age | 12+ |
| Complexity | ●●●○○ Medium (3/5) |
| Tags | hidden role, teams, espionage, cards, deduction |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** F4 / G4
- **Photos:** [155125](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155125.jpg), [155128](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155128.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155125 / 155128：F4 / G4 层 → 风声 The Message（千骐动漫 / 千智）

## Overview

A spy card game based on the film: intelligence cards are both hand and weapon; Lurkers, Military Intelligence and neutral roles race to collect intel.

## Setup

1. Deal identities and characters; deal hands.

## How to play

1. Draw, play cards, pass intelligence (secret, direct, document), receive.
2. Black intelligence: 3 kills you.

## Game end & scoring

- Collect 3 intel of your colour to win; neutrals vary.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** Collect 3 intel of your colour to win; neutrals vary.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Draw, play cards, pass intelligence (secret, direct, document), receive.
3. **Setup check:** Deal identities and characters; deal hands.
4. **The one rule people forget:** 3 black intel = death.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Probes reveal identities.
- Pass black intel to enemies.
- Learn characters gradually.

## Common mistakes

- 3 black intel = death.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** big parties; people who love deduction and guessing identities
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

5. A Red player collects 3 red intel — Red wins.

# Millions of Dollars

> Chinese name: **百万美金** · Doc #148 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../148_%E7%99%BE%E4%B8%87%E7%BE%8E%E9%87%91_Millions_of_Dollars.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/148)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 3–8 (best: 5-7) |
| Play time | 30 min |
| Age | 12+ |
| Complexity | ●●○○○ Light (2/5) |
| Tags | negotiation, bluff, simultaneous choice, loot split |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** F4 / G4
- **Photos:** [155125](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155125.jpg), [155128](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155128.jpg), [204000](../../photos/web/IMG20261127204000.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155125 / 155128：F4 / G4 层 → 百万美金 Millions of Dollars
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 204000：上层右侧（铁网后货架） → 铝箱右侧小盒堆 MILLIONS of dollars

## Overview

Gang loot split: everyone secretly picks a role; duplicated roles are all eliminated; unique roles use abilities. First to 20 million wins.

## Setup

1. Each player takes a family (8 families, same roles, different art): role cards plus matching henchman cards, 5×1M and an aid card.
2. Draw 8 loot cards face down; threat cards aside; give the leader card to one player.

## How to play

1. Plan: reveal a loot card (8–12M total, a 1M or 2M deposit, a favoured role); secretly choose a role face down with your deposit and pass the matching henchman card to the centre.
2. Negotiate: talk, threaten, bribe; quitters take back their deposit. Discard a threat card to peek at a player's role.
3. Rob: resolve Informant → Thug → others. Duplicates are all eliminated; unique roles act.
4. Roles: Thug (always; alone takes a threat card), Driver (always; collects 1M fare from each other sharer), Villain (always; robs the Thug of 2M), Informant (5+; alone recovers deposit and eliminates a role; fined 3M if left alone), Mastermind (7+; alone adds 2M).

## Game end & scoring

- First to 20M wins (some versions 15M at 3–4p), or richest when the 8 loot cards are used.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** First to 20M wins (some versions 15M at 3–4p), or richest when the 8 loot cards are used.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Plan: reveal a loot card (8–12M total, a 1M or 2M deposit, a favoured role); secretly choose a role face down with your deposit and pass the matching henchman card to the centre.
3. **Setup check:** Each player takes a family (8 families, same roles, different art): role cards plus matching henchman cards, 5×1M and an aid card.
4. **The one rule people forget:** Any duplicates are eliminated.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Steer others with promises.
- Popular roles collide; quiet ones survive.
- Watch for bluffs.

## Common mistakes

- Any duplicates are eliminated.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** big parties; talkative groups who enjoy acting and bluffing
- **Not ideal for:** players who dislike conflict or backstabbing

## Advanced guide: teaching script, time budget, phase strategy, extra FAQ

> Summarised by the editor from the rules above for teaching and improving; the rulebook wins on conflicts.

### First-game time budget

- Teaching: about 10 min (weight ●●).
- Play: 30–30 min; budget about 35 min for a first game.
- Total: about 45 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> Gang loot split: everyone secretly picks a role simultaneously; duplicated roles are all eliminated. Survivors split money by role abilities. Most money wins.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Learn others' habits.
- **Mid:** Avoid collisions.
- **Late:** Pick the best role late.

### Extra FAQ

  A: Yes, not binding.
- **Q: Money split?**  
  A: By role abilities.

## Where is it on the shelf?

## Overview

Boxed murder mystery scripts: fixed player counts, playable once: choose roles, read scripts, search clues, discuss and vote.

## Setup

1. Choose the script and assign characters (fixed count).

## How to play

1. Read personal scripts; rounds of clue searching and discussion.

## Game end & scoring

- Vote for the culprit and reveal the truth.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** Vote for the culprit and reveal the truth.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Read personal scripts; rounds of clue searching and discussion.
3. **Setup check:** Choose the script and assign characters (fixed count).
4. **The one rule people forget:** Each script can only be played once.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- The organiser reads rules first.
- Gather the exact player count.
- Role-play your character.

## Common mistakes

- Each script can only be played once.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** big parties; people who love deduction and guessing identities
- **Not ideal for:** when time is short; anyone who has already played the same script

## Advanced guide: teaching script, time budget, phase strategy, extra FAQ

> Summarised by the editor from the rules above for teaching and improving; the rulebook wins on conflicts.

### First-game time budget

- Teaching: about 15 min (weight ●●●).
- Play: 120–240 min; budget about 290 min for a first game.
- Total: about 305 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> Boxed murder mystery: fixed player count, single play. Choose characters, read scripts, search evidence, discuss, vote. The murderer hides; others seek truth.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Read your script carefully.
- **Mid:** Record clues.
- **Late:** Build a timeline before voting.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: Read others' scripts?**  
  A: No.
- **Q: Replay?**  
  A: One-time.

<a id="g150"></a>

# One Night Ultimate Werewolf

> Chinese name: **一夜终极狼人** · Doc #150 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../150_%E4%B8%80%E5%A4%9C%E7%BB%88%E6%9E%81%E7%8B%BC%E4%BA%BA_One_Night_Ultimate_Werewolf.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/150)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 3–10 (best: 5-7) |
| Play time | 10 min |
| Age | 8+ |
| Complexity | ●○○○○ Very light (1/5) |
| Tags | teams, deduction, hidden role, quick, party |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** F5
- **Photos:** [155128](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155128.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155128：F5 层透明收纳箱（蓝框编号标签） → 38 一夜终极狼人…

## Overview

Werewolf in one night: cards = players + 3; roles act in order and swap cards; then discuss and vote simultaneously.

## Setup

1. Use players + 3 role cards; 3 in the centre.

## How to play

1. Night in order: werewolves, minion, seer, robber, troublemaker, drunk, insomniac.
2. Day: discuss, then everyone points simultaneously.

## Game end & scoring

- Village wins if a werewolf dies; werewolves win if none die.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** Village wins if a werewolf dies; werewolves win if none die.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Night in order: werewolves, minion, seer, robber, troublemaker, drunk, insomniac.
3. **Setup check:** Use players + 3 role cards; 3 in the centre.
4. **The one rule people forget:** No eliminations during the game.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Your card may have changed.
- Seer can view two centre cards.
- Wolves may claim troublemaker.

## Common mistakes

- No eliminations during the game.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** big parties; people who love deduction and guessing identities; icebreakers; newcomers and grandparents can learn it instantly; short gaps — waiting for friends, before or after dinner
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

## Detailed supplement: components, worked example, scoring, FAQ, player counts, variants

> Written by the editor from the rules above as a walkthrough; numbers in the example are illustrative. The rulebook in the box always wins.

### Component checklist

- [ ] No complete public component list — use the list in the box.

Before you start:
- Count the components against the rulebook list; sort cards by type — a missing card can break the balance.
- Check tokens, dice and score markers; coins or generic tokens can stand in for missing pieces.
- Hand out reference cards before the first game to save rulebook time.

### Worked example (illustrative)

1. One role each, three in the centre.
2. Night: wolves, seer etc.
3. Robber and troublemaker swap.
4. Discuss and vote.
5. Kill a wolf: village wins.

### Scoring example

- Village wins by killing a wolf.

### Rules FAQ / rulings

- **Q: What if no werewolves are among players?**  
  A: If both are in the centre, the village wins only if nobody dies (each player gets exactly one vote).

### Player-count differences

- 3–10.
- Best with: 5-7 players.

### Official variants & expansions

- Daybreak etc.

## Advanced guide: teaching script, time budget, phase strategy, extra FAQ

> Summarised by the editor from the rules above for teaching and improving; the rulebook wins on conflicts.

### First-game time budget

- Teaching: about 5 min (weight ●).
- Play: 10–10 min; budget about 10 min for a first game.
- Total: about 15 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> One-night werewolf. Three extra role cards in the centre. Night actions in order: werewolves, seer, robber, troublemaker, drunk... At dawn discuss and vote simultaneously. Village wins by killing a werewolf; if no werewolves are among players, the village wins only if nobody dies.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Remember your night info.
- **Mid:** Share info and find contradictions.
- **Late:** Consider wolves in the centre.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: No werewolves among players?**  
  A: Village wins only if nobody dies (each gets one vote).
- **Q: Can my role change?**  
  A: Robber or Troublemaker may swap it; your final card counts.

# Mall of Horror

> Chinese name: **僵尸商场 Mall of Horror** · Doc #151 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../151_%E5%83%B5%E5%B0%B8%E5%95%86%E5%9C%BA_Mall_of_Horror.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/151)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 3–6 (best: 5-6) |
| Play time | 60 min |
| Age | 14+ |
| Complexity | ●●○○○ Light (2/5) |
| Tags | voting, negotiation, traitor, survival |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** F5
- **Photos:** [155128](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155128.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155128：F5 层透明收纳箱（蓝框编号标签） → 01 僵尸商城

## Overview

Zombies surround a mall: choose rooms secretly, then vote who gets thrown out when zombies break into the weakest room.

## Setup

1. Place characters (Pin-up, Tough Guy, Gunman) in rooms.

## How to play

1. Parking search, elect the chief of security, roll zombie arrivals, secretly choose moves, zombie attack votes.

## Game end & scoring

- When few survivors remain, total character values; highest wins.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** When few survivors remain, total character values; highest wins.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Parking search, elect the chief of security, roll zombie arrivals, secretly choose moves, zombie attack votes.
3. **Setup check:** Place characters (Pin-up, Tough Guy, Gunman) in rooms.
4. **The one rule people forget:** Tough Guy's ability does not work in the parking lot.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Security chief has information.
- Tough Guy counts double.
- Alliances and betrayal are the fun.

## Common mistakes

- Tough Guy's ability does not work in the parking lot.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** talkative groups who enjoy acting and bluffing
- **Not ideal for:** players who dislike conflict or backstabbing

# Guess Who?

> Chinese name: **猜猜我是谁 Guess Who** · Doc #153 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../153_%E7%8C%9C%E7%8C%9C%E6%88%91%E6%98%AF%E8%B0%81_Guess_Who.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/153)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 2 (best: 2) |
| Play time | 10–20 min |
| Age | 6+ |
| Complexity | ●○○○○ Very light (1/5) |
| Tags | 2-player, deduction, kids, family, classic |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** F5
- **Photos:** [155128](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155128.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155128：F5 下方与左侧 → 猜猜我是谁 Guess Who（红色翻板）

## Overview

Two-player yes/no deduction: flip down faces that do not match and guess the opponent's mystery person first.

## Setup

1. Each player draws a mystery person card.

## How to play

1. Ask one yes/no question; flip down non-matching faces.
2. Use a turn to guess; a wrong guess loses.

## Game end & scoring

- First correct guess wins.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** First correct guess wins.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Ask one yes/no question; flip down non-matching faces.
3. **Setup check:** Each player draws a mystery person card.
4. **The one rule people forget:** A wrong guess loses immediately.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Ask questions that split half.
- Do not guess early.
- Gamble when behind.

## Common mistakes

- A wrong guess loses immediately.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** couples / head-to-head duels; people who love deduction and guessing identities; families with young children; short gaps — waiting for friends, before or after dinner
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

- Hand out reference cards before the first game to save rulebook time.

### Worked example (illustrative)

1. Pick a secret character.
2. Ask yes/no questions.
3. Flip down non-matching faces.
4. Narrow down.
5. Guess correctly to win.

### Scoring example

- First to guess wins.

### Rules FAQ / rulings

- See “Common mistakes” above; anything else — check the rulebook.

### Player-count differences

- 2.
- Best with: 2 players.

### Official variants & expansions

- Team play.

- Total: about 30 min.

- **Mid:** Watch their board.
- **Late:** Guess when sure.

  A: You lose.

# Aeroplane Chess

> Chinese name: **飞行棋** · Doc #154 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../154_%E9%A3%9E%E8%A1%8C%E6%A3%8B_Aeroplane_Chess.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/154)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 2–4 (best: 4) |
| Play time | 20–40 min |
| Age | 5+ |
| Complexity | ●○○○○ Very light (1/5) |
| Tags | classic, dice, kids, family |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** F5
- **Photos:** [155128](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155128.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155128：F5 下方与左侧 → 飞行棋

## Overview

Chinese classic dice race: roll a 6 to take off, jump on own colour, fly shortcuts and bump opponents home.

## Setup

1. Four planes per player in the hangar.

## How to play

1. Roll 6 to launch; move by die.
2. Landing on your colour jumps ahead; flight lines shortcut.
3. Landing on opponents sends them home.

## Game end & scoring

- First to bring all 4 planes home wins.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** First to bring all 4 planes home wins.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Roll 6 to launch; move by die.
3. **Setup check:** Four planes per player in the hangar.
4. **The one rule people forget:** Home usually needs an exact roll.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Launch several planes.
- Stay out of 1–6 range behind opponents.
- Agree house rules first.

## Common mistakes

- Home usually needs an exact roll.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** families with young children
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

# Jenga

> Chinese name: **叠叠乐 Jenga** · Doc #155 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../155_%E5%8F%A0%E5%8F%A0%E4%B9%90_Jenga.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/155)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 1–8 (best: 2-4) |
| Play time | 5–15 min |
| Age | 6+ |
| Complexity | ●○○○○ Very light (1/5) |
| Tags | dexterity, balance, family, party, classic |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** F5
- **Photos:** [155128](../../photos/web/IMG20261127155128.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 155128：F5 下方与左侧 → 叠叠乐 Jenga 类木块（透明箱散装 1 套 + 黄色托盘数字木块 1 套；原 #49 已解决）

## Overview

Build an 18-level tower; take turns removing a block with one hand and placing it on top; whoever topples it loses.

## Setup

1. Build 18 levels of 3 blocks, alternating directions.

## How to play

1. Use one hand at a time.
2. Remove a block below the top completed level and place it on top.
3. Your turn ends after 10 seconds or when the next player touches the tower.

## Game end & scoring

- The player who makes the tower fall loses.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** The player who makes the tower fall loses.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Use one hand at a time.
3. **Setup check:** Build 18 levels of 3 blocks, alternating directions.
4. **The one rule people forget:** Do not take from the top incomplete level or the one below it.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Probe for loose blocks.
- Centre blocks keep balance.
- Finish the top level properly.

## Common mistakes

- Do not take from the top incomplete level or the one below it.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** big parties; families with young children; icebreakers; newcomers and grandparents can learn it instantly; short gaps — waiting for friends, before or after dinner; fans of speed and excitement
- **Not ideal for:** people who want to think slowly and quietly

- Check tokens, dice and score markers; coins or generic tokens can stand in for missing pieces.
- Hand out reference cards before the first game to save rulebook time.

2. Remove one block with one hand.
3. Place it on top.
4. He topples the tower.
5. Toppler loses.

### Scoring example

- Toppler loses.

### Rules FAQ / rulings

- **Q: Two hands?**  
  A: One hand only.

### Player-count differences

- 1+.
- Best with: 2-4 players.

### Official variants & expansions

- See rulebook.

- Total: about 25 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> Build an 18-level tower. Take turns removing a block (below the top level) with one hand and placing it on top. Topple it and you lose.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Pull loose middle blocks.
- **Mid:** Watch the centre of gravity.
- **Late:** Be careful late.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: Two hands?**  
  A: One hand only.
- **Q: Can I pull from the top?**  
  A: Not from the top level or the one below if incomplete.

## Where is it on the shelf?

- Original note (Chinese): 用户补充（不在照片中） → 位置待确认（请在店内询问）

## Overview

Large social deduction game run by a Storyteller: good players hunt the Demon; every role is unique, the dead stay in the game with one ghost vote, and drunk or poisoned players may get false information.

## Setup

1. The Storyteller picks a script (Trouble Brewing for beginners, 5–15 players) and builds the character mix by player count (e.g. 7 players: 5 Townsfolk, 0 Outsiders, 1 Minion, 1 Demon).
2. Players sit in a circle and secretly draw unique character tokens; the Storyteller records seats and roles in the Grimoire.
3. The Drunk is given a Townsfolk token and does not know they are the Drunk.

## How to play

1. First night: with 7+ players the Minions and Demon learn each other; the Demon learns 3 good characters not in play to bluff as; first-night characters receive information in order.
2. Day: deaths are announced (roles stay hidden); open and private discussion.
3. Nominations: only living players nominate, once per day each, and each player can be nominated once per day; votes of at least half the living players put someone on the block; a later nominee needs more votes to replace them; a tie means no execution.
4. At most one execution per day; then night falls. Each later night the Demon kills and other characters act in order.

## Game end & scoring

- Good wins when the Demon dies (and no new Demon is created, e.g. by the Scarlet Woman).
- Evil wins when only 2 players are alive and the Demon lives, or via special conditions such as the Saint being executed.
- The dead win or lose with their team.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** Good wins when the Demon dies (and no new Demon is created, e.g. by the Scarlet Woman).
2. **What you do on a turn:** First night: with 7+ players the Minions and Demon learn each other; the Demon learns 3 good characters not in play to bluff as; first-night characters receive information in order.
3. **Setup check:** The Storyteller picks a script (Trouble Brewing for beginners, 5–15 players) and builds the character mix by player count (e.g. 7 players: 5 Townsfolk, 0 Outsiders, 1 Minion, 1 Demon).
4. **The one rule people forget:** Dead players cannot nominate and have only one ghost vote for the whole game.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Cross-check information: you might be poisoned or the Drunk.
- Good players share information early; evil players prepare a bluff.
- Do not go quiet after death — ghost votes often decide the final day.
- Start new groups on Trouble Brewing.

## Common mistakes

- Dead players cannot nominate and have only one ghost vote for the whole game.
- A tied vote executes no one.
- You need a Storyteller who knows the rules well.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** big parties; talkative groups who enjoy acting and bluffing; people who love deduction and guessing identities
- **Not ideal for:** players who dislike conflict or backstabbing

## Detailed supplement: components, worked example, scoring, FAQ, player counts, variants

> Written by the editor from the rules above as a walkthrough; numbers in the example are illustrative. The rulebook in the box always wins.

### Component checklist

From the Chinese doc (original wording):

- [ ] 说书人用的魔典（Grimoire）和角色标记、提示标记
- [ ] 镇广场（生命标记、幽灵票标记）
- [ ] 角色表、夜晚行动顺序表、剧本册
- [ ] 说明书

Before you start:
- Count the components against the rulebook list; sort cards by type — a missing card can break the balance.
- Check tokens, dice and score markers; coins or generic tokens can stand in for missing pieces.
- Hand out reference cards before the first game to save rulebook time.

### Worked example (illustrative)

1. 8 players (Trouble Brewing): 5 Townsfolk, 1 Outsider, 1 Minion, 1 Demon.
2. First night: Minion and Demon meet (7+ players) and the Demon gets 3 bluffs; the Washerwoman learns one of seats 3 or 6 is the Empath.
3. Day 1: seat 4 nominates seat 7; 5 hands (8 alive, 4 needed); seat 7 is executed — a Townsfolk.
4. Night 2: the Imp kills seat 2; the Empath learns one evil neighbour.
5. Day 3: cross-checked info points at seat 5; dead seat 7 spends the ghost vote; seat 5 is the Imp and is executed — good wins.

### Scoring example

- No points: good wins when the Demon dies; evil wins with 2 alive and the Demon alive; special conditions like the Saint or Mayor apply.

### Rules FAQ / rulings

- **Q: Can the dead vote?**  
  A: Yes, once per game with their ghost vote; they cannot nominate.
- **Q: What about a tie?**  
  A: A tie means no execution that day.
- **Q: Can the Storyteller lie?**  
  A: Only when rules allow, e.g. to poisoned or drunk players.

### Player-count differences

- 5–6 players suit small scripts; Minions and Demon meet from 7 players; 10+ players add Minions; 8–12 is the sweet spot.
- Best with: 8-12 players.

### Official variants & expansions

- Three base scripts (Trouble Brewing, Bad Moon Rising, Sects & Violets), teensy scripts, Travellers and many community scripts.

## Advanced guide: teaching script, time budget, phase strategy, extra FAQ

> Summarised by the editor from the rules above for teaching and improving; the rulebook wins on conflicts.

### First-game time budget

- Teaching: about 15 min (weight ●●●).
- Play: 60–120 min; budget about 145 min for a first game.
- Total: about 160 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> A Storyteller runs the game; everyone has a unique role. Good finds and executes the Demon; evil wins when only 2 players live. At night the Demon kills and roles get info; by day discuss, nominate and vote. The dead stay in, keep talking and have one ghost vote. Info can be wrong due to drunkenness or poison.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Good: share info carefully.
- **Mid:** Cross-check info and death patterns.
- **Late:** At final three, every vote matters — use ghost votes.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: Can the dead vote?**  
  A: One ghost vote per game.
- **Q: Vote threshold?**  
  A: ≥ half the living; tie means no execution.

---

# Blade's Rose

> Chinese name: **血与刃的白蔷薇 Blade's Rose** · Doc #157 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../157_%E8%A1%80%E4%B8%8E%E5%88%83%E7%9A%84%E7%99%BD%E8%94%B7%E8%96%87_Blades_Rose.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/157)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 5–10 (best: 7-9) |
| Play time | 20–40 min |
| Age | 8+ |
| Complexity | ●●○○○ Light (2/5) |
| Tags | hidden teams, deduction, teams, party, quick |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** none on the photo — inferred area:  *(inferred)*
- Original note (Chinese): 用户补充（不在照片中） → 位置待确认（请在店内询问）

## Overview

Everyone holds 3 role cards — any red card makes you a Blade. Each round players secretly add cards to the ritual; if a blade is revealed, the white cards are killed; the White Rose revealed with a blade means instant Blade victory.

## Setup

1. Use the player board and role cards for the player count; deal 3 role cards to each player and set centre cards per the rules.
2. Hand out the White Crystal spell cards as the rulebook says.
3. Night: eye-icon roles (White Rose, Bishop, Twin Blade, Great Blade) wake in order; the Twin Blade may swap the Ghost with the centre Twin Blade.

## How to play

1. The coin holder passes the gold coin to a player who still has crystals; that player reveals and resolves one crystal.
2. From the coin holder, each player in order may play one role card face down (or pass).
3. Shuffle the played cards and reveal; discard any Ghost.
4. If any blade is revealed, all revealed white cards go to the kill area; otherwise they go to the sacrifice area.

## Game end & scoring

- White Rose revealed together with a blade: the Blades win instantly.
- White wins if the White Rose has been played safely and sacrifices reach the board threshold before the final round.
- Blades win on reaching the kill threshold, or when the final round ends without a White victory.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** White Rose revealed together with a blade: the Blades win instantly.
2. **What you do on a turn:** The coin holder passes the gold coin to a player who still has crystals; that player reveals and resolves one crystal.
3. **Setup check:** Use the player board and role cards for the player count; deal 3 role cards to each player and set centre cards per the rules.
4. **The one rule people forget:** Your team depends on all 3 cards in hand.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- The White Rose should go in on a round that looks clean.
- Passing is information too.
- Blades can play white cards first to look innocent.

## Common mistakes

- Your team depends on all 3 cards in hand.
- A revealed Ghost is simply discarded.
- The sacrifice threshold must be reached before the final round.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** big parties; people who love deduction and guessing identities
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

## Detailed supplement: components, worked example, scoring, FAQ, player counts, variants

> Written by the editor from the rules above as a walkthrough; numbers in the example are illustrative. The rulebook in the box always wins.

### Component checklist

- [ ] 角色牌
- [ ] 12 张白水晶魔法书技能卡
- [ ] 1 枚金币（行动指示）

Before you start:
- Count the components against the rulebook list; sort cards by type — a missing card can break the balance.
- Check tokens, dice and score markers; coins or generic tokens can stand in for missing pieces.
- Hand out reference cards before the first game to save rulebook time.

### Worked example (illustrative)

1. 7 players, 3 cards each; Ming holds one red card, so he is a Blade.
2. Night: the White Rose and Bishop wake; the Twin Blade swaps the Ghost with the centre Twin Blade.
3. Round 1: the coin goes to Li, who resolves a crystal; 4 cards are played face down.
4. Revealed: 3 white + 1 Ghost, no blade — 3 white cards go to sacrifice.
5. Round 3: the White Rose comes out alongside a Great Blade — Blades win instantly.

### Scoring example

- Threshold-based: White needs the sacrifice threshold before the last round; Blades need the kill threshold or to survive to the end.

### Rules FAQ / rulings

- **Q: Two white and one red?**  
  A: Blade — any red card makes you a Blade.
- **Q: Does the Ghost cause kills?**  
  A: No, it is discarded when revealed.

### Player-count differences

- 5–10 players with a board per count; 7–9 is best.
- Best with: 7-9 players.

### Official variants & expansions

- A 2.0 edition adjusts some roles and spell cards.

## Advanced guide: teaching script, time budget, phase strategy, extra FAQ

> Summarised by the editor from the rules above for teaching and improving; the rulebook wins on conflicts.

### First-game time budget

- Teaching: about 10 min (weight ●●).
- Play: 20–40 min; budget about 50 min for a first game.
- Total: about 60 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> Each player holds 3 role cards; any red means Blade side. Each round play cards secretly into a ritual; blades kill, roses and blades are revealed together. Deduce the Blade side; win per rulebook conditions.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Watch talk and plays.
- **Mid:** Deduce from reveals.
- **Late:** Focus votes late.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: Is there a 2.0?**  
  A: Yes, rules may differ.
- **Q: Players?**  
  A: 5–10.

# Betrayal at House on the Hill 3rd Edition

> Chinese name: **林中小屋第三版 Betrayal at House on the Hill 3rd** · Doc #159 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../159_%E6%9E%97%E4%B8%AD%E5%B0%8F%E5%B1%8B%E7%AC%AC%E4%B8%89%E7%89%88_Betrayal_at_House_on_the_Hill_3rd.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/159)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 3–6 (best: 4-5) |
| Play time | 60–90 min |
| Age | 12+ |
| Complexity | ●●●○○ Medium (3/5) |
| Tags | horror, exploration, traitor, semi-co-op, story, dice rolling |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** none on the photo — inferred area: shelf behind the wire grid, upper level, left stack (no code) *(inferred)*
- **Photos:** [204000](../../photos/web/IMG20261127204000.jpg)
- **Close-up crops:** [c9](../../photos/crops/c9_204000_left.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 204000：上层左侧（铁网后货架） → 黑色 林中小屋 3rd EDITION 盒

## Overview

Explore a haunted house together; omens build up until the haunt starts, then a scenario reveals a traitor or special situation and each side follows secret goals. 50 haunts.

## Setup

1. Place the 3 starting tiles; shuffle the other room tiles.
2. Each player takes a character and sets Might, Speed, Sanity and Knowledge to their start values.
3. Separate Event, Item and Omen cards; choose a Scenario card (it decides which haunt and who the traitor is).

## How to play

1. Move up to your Speed; discovering a new room ends your turn immediately (3rd-edition rule).
2. Room symbols: draw an Event, Item or Omen.
3. Haunt roll: after each Omen, roll as many dice as Omens revealed; 5 or more starts the haunt; the last Omen always starts it.
4. After the haunt: read the introduction and setup aloud, then traitor and heroes read their own secret goals separately.

## Game end & scoring

- The side that completes its haunt goal wins.
- Monsters are usually only stunned, not killed, unless the haunt says otherwise.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** The side that completes its haunt goal wins.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Move up to your Speed; discovering a new room ends your turn immediately (3rd-edition rule).
3. **Setup check:** Place the 3 starting tiles; shuffle the other room tiles.
4. **The one rule people forget:** Draw cards only when a room is first revealed.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Collect items before the haunt.
- Track omens to predict when the haunt arrives.
- Heroes should split tasks quickly.

## Common mistakes

- Draw cards only when a room is first revealed.
- No one can die before the haunt.
- Stealing items is off by default in 3rd edition.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** most board gamers
- **Not ideal for:** players who dislike conflict or backstabbing

## Detailed supplement: components, worked example, scoring, FAQ, player counts, variants

- [ ] 74 张卡牌（事件、物品、预兆）
- [ ] 5 张剧本卡（Scenario）
- [ ] 8 颗骰子（每面 0、1、2）
- [ ] 6 块双面角色板、6 个角色模型和底座
- [ ] 玩家参考卡、怪物参考卡、各种指示物

Before you start:
- Count the components against the rulebook list; sort cards by type — a missing card can break the balance.
- Check tokens, dice and score markers; coins or generic tokens can stand in for missing pieces.
- Hand out reference cards before the first game to save rulebook time.

### Worked example (illustrative)

1. 4 players start on the three starting tiles after choosing a Scenario card.
2. Wang opens a door, reveals the Library, draws an item — turn over.
3. Third omen: roll 3 dice for 2+2+1=5 — the haunt starts.
4. The Scenario card gives the haunt and the traitor; everyone reads the intro aloud.
5. The heroes complete the ritual from Secrets of Survival and win.

### Scoring example

- No points: whoever completes their haunt goal wins.

### Rules FAQ / rulings

- **Q: How is the haunt roll done?**  
  A: Roll dice equal to omens revealed; 5+ starts the haunt.
- **Q: Can you die before the haunt?**  
  A: No.
- **Q: Mix with 2nd edition?**  
  A: Not recommended; rules and haunt books differ.

### Player-count differences

- 3–6 players; 4–5 is best.
- Best with: 4-5 players.

### Official variants & expansions

- Expansion: The Werewolf's Journey: Blood on the Moon.

## Advanced guide: teaching script, time budget, phase strategy, extra FAQ

> Summarised by the editor from the rules above for teaching and improving; the rulebook wins on conflicts.

### First-game time budget

- Teaching: about 15 min (weight ●●●).
- Play: 60–90 min; budget about 110 min for a first game.
- Total: about 125 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> Explore a haunted house. After drawing an omen, roll dice equal to the omen count; 5+ triggers the haunt (the last omen triggers automatically). The scenario card chooses the haunt; reveal the traitor or special setup, then sides pursue secret goals. Discovering a room ends your movement.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Explore and grab items early.
- **Mid:** Follow the haunt objective.
- **Late:** Concentrate resources late.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: Steal items?**  
  A: Not by default.
- **Q: Haunt roll?**  
  A: Dice = omens; 5+ triggers.

# Warhammer Quest: The Adventure Card Game

> Chinese name: **战锤任务：冒险卡牌游戏 Warhammer Quest ACG** · Doc #160 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../160_%E6%88%98%E9%94%A4%E4%BB%BB%E5%8A%A1_Warhammer_Quest_Adventure_Card_Game.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/160)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 1–4 (best: 2-4) |
| Play time | 30–60 min |
| Age | 14+ |
| Complexity | ●●●○○ Medium (3/5) |
| Tags | co-op, dungeon, dice rolling, campaign, RPG |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** none on the photo — inferred area: shelf behind the wire grid, upper level, left stack (no code) *(inferred)*
- **Photos:** [204000](../../photos/web/IMG20261127204000.jpg)
- **Close-up crops:** [c9](../../photos/crops/c9_204000_left.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 204000：上层左侧（铁网后货架） → 最下面 Warhammer Quest 棕色盒

## Overview

Fully co-operative dungeon card game: each hero has only four action cards — Explore, Rest, Aid and Attack — rolled with custom dice; explore locations, fight enemies and race the peril track across a five-quest campaign.

## Setup

1. Choose a quest; build location, enemy and loot decks per the quest sheet.
2. Each player picks a hero (Ironbreaker, Warrior Priest, Bright Wizard, Waywatcher) with 4 action cards; fewer players means more hit points; solo usually runs 2 heroes.
3. Place the peril token, choose a party leader, reveal the first location and spawn enemies.

## How to play

1. Hero phase: each hero performs 1 action — Explore (progress tokens), Rest (heal), Aid (give success tokens to another hero, max 2 per card) or Attack (damage an engaged enemy; ranged can hit shadows).
2. Used action cards are exhausted; one action per hero readies all of them.
3. Every action you take triggers a black die from each enemy engaged with you.
4. Enemy phase, then location phase (resolve effects; travel when fully explored), then the peril token advances.

## Game end & scoring

- Complete the quest's final objective (e.g. defeat the nemesis) to win as a team.
- Lose if all heroes are defeated or a quest failure condition triggers.
- In campaign mode heroes level up between quests.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** Complete the quest's final objective (e.g. defeat the nemesis) to win as a team.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Hero phase: each hero performs 1 action — Explore (progress tokens), Rest (heal), Aid (give success tokens to another hero, max 2 per card) or Attack (damage an engaged enemy; ranged can hit shadows).
3. **Setup check:** Choose a quest; build location, enemy and loot decks per the quest sheet.
4. **The one rule people forget:** Each engaged enemy rolls a black die whenever you act.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Kill high-attack enemies before exploring.
- Aid tokens on a teammate's key action are strong.
- Plan your ready timing.

## Common mistakes

- Each engaged enemy rolls a black die whenever you act.
- Max 2 success tokens per action card.
- 3-player: first player acts twice; 2-player: each acts twice.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** friends who prefer working together
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

## Detailed supplement: components, worked example, scoring, FAQ, player counts, variants

> Written by the editor from the rules above as a walkthrough; numbers in the example are illustrative. The rulebook in the box always wins.

### Component checklist

From the Chinese doc (original wording):

- [ ] 4 名英雄：矮人碎铁者（Ironbreaker）、战斗祭司、光明巫师、木精灵守望者；每人 1 张英雄卡和 **4 张行动卡**
- [ ] 白色英雄骰、黑色敌人骰
- [ ] 任务表、地点牌、敌人牌、战利品、状态等卡牌
- [ ] 进度指示物、成功指示物、伤害指示物、危险（peril）指示物、队长指示物

Before you start:
- Count the components against the rulebook list; sort cards by type — a missing card can break the balance.
- Check tokens, dice and score markers; coins or generic tokens can stand in for missing pieces.
- Hand out reference cards before the first game to save rulebook time.

### Worked example (illustrative)

1. 2 players, one hero each acting twice per round: Ironbreaker and Bright Wizard.
2. The first location spawns 2 enemies; the Ironbreaker rolls 3 successes and kills a goblin.
3. The Wizard explores for 2 progress; an engaged enemy's black die deals 1 damage.
4. Fully explored — travel on, discarding normal enemies in the shadows.
5. Defeat the nemesis at the final location: quest complete.

### Scoring example

- No points: complete the quest objective; progress carries over in a campaign.

### Rules FAQ / rulings

- **Q: All actions exhausted?**  
  A: Each hero has one action that readies all cards.
- **Q: How do criticals work?**  
  A: Count a success and roll again, can chain.

### Player-count differences

- 1–4; solo runs 2 heroes; 4 players gives the best synergy.
- Best with: 2-4 players.

### Official variants & expansions

- Hero packs (e.g. Witch Hunter, Troll Slayer) and delve mode.

## Advanced guide: teaching script, time budget, phase strategy, extra FAQ

> Summarised by the editor from the rules above for teaching and improving; the rulebook wins on conflicts.

### First-game time budget

- Teaching: about 15 min (weight ●●●).
- Play: 30–60 min; budget about 70 min for a first game.
- Total: about 85 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> Fully co-op dungeon cards. Each hero has four actions: explore, rest, aid, attack; roll for successes. Explore locations, clear enemies, manage peril, complete the quest.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Clear big threats first.
- **Mid:** Balance aid and rest.
- **Late:** Focus on the quest late.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: Exhausted actions?**  
  A: Rest to ready.
- **Q: Hero death?**  
  A: May lose.

# Apocalypse Revelation (zombie survival co-op)

> Chinese name: **末日启示录（僵尸生存合作）** · Doc #162 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../162_%E6%9C%AB%E6%97%A5%E5%90%AF%E7%A4%BA%E5%BD%95.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/162)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 1–4 (best: 2-4) |
| Play time | 60–90 min |
| Age | 14+ |
| Complexity | ●●●○○ Medium (3/5) |
| Tags | co-op, survival, card-driven, exploration, horror |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** none on the photo — inferred area: shelf behind the wire grid, upper level, middle (no code) *(inferred)*
- **Photos:** [204000](../../photos/web/IMG20261127204000.jpg)
- **Close-up crops:** [c10](../../photos/crops/c10_204000_mid.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 204000：上层中部（铁网后货架） → 下方 黑色盒（蘑菇云图标）

## Overview

Small-box American-style co-op: each character has a personal deck; each round roll for monster spawns, draw, take 4 actions (move, draw, play, card action, scavenge) and complete the scenario. An empty deck means death.

## Setup

1. Choose a scenario and lay out map tiles in a grid face down as it requires.
2. Prepare scavenge and monster decks.
3. Each player takes a character and its deck and draws 4 cards; set the hunger die to 1.

## How to play

1. Monster spawn: roll 2d6; every revealed tile whose number equals the sum spawns monsters.
2. Draw 1 card from your own deck (hand limit 10).
3. Take exactly 4 actions: move 1 tile (reveal unexplored tiles), draw, play a card (gear limit 4), take a card action, or scavenge.
4. Resolve location effects; then engaged monsters attack you.

## Game end & scoring

- Complete the scenario objective to win together.
- If a character dies (no health, or deck runs out) the game is normally lost.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** Complete the scenario objective to win together.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Monster spawn: roll 2d6; every revealed tile whose number equals the sum spawns monsters.
3. **Setup check:** Choose a scenario and lay out map tiles in a grid face down as it requires.
4. **The one rule people forget:** Exactly 4 actions per player.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Avoid tiles with frequently rolled numbers.
- Draw to find weapons, but watch your deck size.
- Combine play-a-card with a card action for combos.

## Common mistakes

- Exactly 4 actions per player.
- An empty deck kills your character.
- Stealth check: stealth minus the 2d6 modifier must reach the number of monster tokens.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** friends who prefer working together
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

## Detailed supplement: components, worked example, scoring, FAQ, player counts, variants

> Written by the editor from the rules above as a walkthrough; numbers in the example are illustrative. The rulebook in the box always wins.

### Component checklist

From the Chinese doc (original wording):

- [ ] 地图板块（右上角印数字，用来判定刷怪）
- [ ] 角色牌和各角色专属牌库（如擅长肉搏的消防员、会治疗的外科医生、擅长搜索和设陷阱的机械师等）
- [ ] 怪物牌库 / 怪物标记、拾荒牌库
- [ ] 两颗六面大骰子、记录饥饿等级的小骰子
- [ ] 剧本卡、规则书

Before you start:
- Count the components against the rulebook list; sort cards by type — a missing card can break the balance.
- Check tokens, dice and score markers; coins or generic tokens can stand in for missing pieces.
- Hand out reference cards before the first game to save rulebook time.

### Worked example (illustrative)

1. 2 players, scenario 1, map face down.
2. Spawn: a 7 is rolled; every revealed 7 tile spawns.
3. Firefighter: move, move (reveal), equip an axe, scavenge food.
4. Surgeon heals a teammate and uses her special action.
5. Monsters attack; in round 5 the supplies are collected — victory.

### Scoring example

- No points: complete the scenario; a death (including empty deck) loses.

### Rules FAQ / rulings

- **Q: Empty deck?**  
  A: The character dies, usually losing the game.
- **Q: Stealth check?**  
  A: Stealth minus 2d6 must reach the number of monster tokens.

### Player-count differences

- Player count per the box.
- Best with: 2-4 players.

### Official variants & expansions

- A dozen-plus scenarios and 4 monster decks.

## Advanced guide: teaching script, time budget, phase strategy, extra FAQ

> Summarised by the editor from the rules above for teaching and improving; the rulebook wins on conflicts.

### First-game time budget

- Teaching: about 15 min (weight ●●●).
- Play: 60–90 min; budget about 110 min for a first game.
- Total: about 125 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> A small co-op survival game. Each character has a unique deck. Each round roll to spawn zombies, draw, and take 4 actions (move, draw, play, card actions). Lose at 0 HP or an empty deck.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Spend actions wisely.
- **Mid:** Watch your deck size.
- **Late:** Rush the objective late.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: Empty deck?**  
  A: Loss.
- **Q: Solo?**  
  A: Yes, 1–4.

# Legendary Encounters: Alien

> Chinese name: **传奇冲突：异形 Legendary Encounters: Alien** · Doc #164 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../164_%E4%BC%A0%E5%A5%87%E5%86%B2%E7%AA%81_%E5%BC%82%E5%BD%A2_Legendary_Encounters_Alien.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/164)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 1–5 (best: 2-4) |
| Play time | 45–60 min |
| Age | 17+ |
| Complexity | ●●●○○ Medium (3/5) |
| Tags | co-op, deck-building, sci-fi, horror, hidden role |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** none on the photo — inferred area: shelf behind the wire grid, upper level, middle (no code) *(inferred)*
- **Photos:** [204000](../../photos/web/IMG20261127204000.jpg)
- **Close-up crops:** [c10](../../photos/crops/c10_204000_mid.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 204000：上层中部（铁网后货架） → 黑色长盒 傳奇衝突 異形卡牌構築遊戲

## Overview

Co-operative deck-builder: use Attack to scan and kill aliens advancing from the Hive, use Recruit to buy cards from the Barracks, and complete three film-based objectives. Facehuggers burst out after two turns.

## Setup

1. Build three objective mini-decks (9, 11 and 13 cards), each with one Drone per player.
2. Shuffle the Barracks (4 characters × 14 cards) and reveal 5 in HQ.
3. Each player shuffles 7 Specialists, 5 Grunts and 1 Role card and draws 6.

## How to play

1. Play cards for Attack and Recruit points and their effects.
2. Scan face-down Hive cards in the Complex, or attack revealed aliens in the Combat Zone.
3. Recruit from HQ (refill immediately).
4. Hive phase: a new Hive card enters and pushes the others forward; Strike phase: aliens in the Combat Zone hit the active player. Discard and draw 6.

## Game end & scoring

- Win by completing all three objectives in order.
- Lose if any player is killed (base rules) or another objective failure condition triggers.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** Win by completing all three objectives in order.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Play cards for Attack and Recruit points and their effects.
3. **Setup check:** Build three objective mini-decks (9, 11 and 13 cards), each with one Drone per player.
4. **The one rule people forget:** Facehuggers burst after 2 turns.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Scan before committing attacks.
- Do not let the Combat Zone fill up.
- Split roles between attackers and recruiters.

## Common mistakes

- Facehuggers burst after 2 turns.
- Base rules: one death loses the game for everyone.
- Gory theme, 17+.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** friends who prefer working together; people who love deduction and guessing identities
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

## Detailed supplement: components, worked example, scoring, FAQ, player counts, variants

> Written by the editor from the rules above as a walkthrough; numbers in the example are illustrative. The rulebook in the box always wins.

### Component checklist

From the Chinese doc (original wording):

- [ ] 起始牌：每人 7 张专员（Specialist）+ 5 张士兵（Grunt）+ 1 张角色（Role）牌
- [ ] 兵营（Barracks）：4 个角色 × 14 张 = 56 张可招募的牌
- [ ] 巢穴（Hive）牌库：异形、事件、抱脸虫等
- [ ] 三个**目标**迷你牌堆（分别对应三部电影的剧情目标）
- [ ] 游戏垫：5 个综合体（Complex）房间格、战斗区、指挥部（HQ）等

Before you start:
- Count the components against the rulebook list; sort cards by type — a missing card can break the balance.
- Check tokens, dice and score markers; coins or generic tokens can stand in for missing pieces.
- Hand out reference cards before the first game to save rulebook time.

### Worked example (illustrative)

1. 3 players, first-film objectives.
2. Lin plays 3 Specialists for 3 Attack and 2 Recruit and scans the outer card: a Drone.
3. She recruits a 2-cost card; HQ refills.
4. Hive phase pushes the Drone into the Combat Zone; Strike deals 2 damage.
5. The next player kills it; the team works through all three objectives.

### Scoring example

- No points: complete three objectives together.

### Rules FAQ / rulings

- **Q: When does a facehugger burst?**  
  A: After 2 turns.
- **Q: Someone dies?**  
  A: Base: everyone loses; Player Aliens variant: they become an alien.

### Player-count differences

- 1–5; best at 2–4.
- Best with: 2-4 players.

### Official variants & expansions

- Player Aliens and Hidden Agenda variants; expansions.

## Advanced guide: teaching script, time budget, phase strategy, extra FAQ

> Summarised by the editor from the rules above for teaching and improving; the rulebook wins on conflicts.

### First-game time budget

- Teaching: about 15 min (weight ●●●).
- Play: 45–60 min; budget about 70 min for a first game.
- Total: about 85 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> Co-op deckbuilding: use attack to scan and kill aliens advancing from the hive, recruit points to buy cards from HQ. Complete three film objectives. Beware facehuggers and hidden aliens.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Buy balanced recruit/attack cards.
- **Mid:** Scan unknowns promptly.
- **Late:** Focus attacks late.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: Facehuggers?**  
  A: Per rulebook, may cause chestbursters.
- **Q: Objective decks?**  
  A: 9/11/13 cards, 1 Drone per player.

## Overview

Chinese Monopoly-style game, electronic banking edition (card reader instead of paper money) with a dice tower: roll around the board, buy property, collect rent, build and bankrupt opponents. Rules follow common Monopoly practice; check the rulebook.

## Setup

1. Each player takes a token and starting money (loaded onto their bank card in the electronic version).
2. The bank holds the property cards; check the card reader's batteries.

## How to play

1. Roll two dice and move.
2. Unowned property: buy it (or auction per the rulebook); owned property: pay rent; complete colour sets to build houses and hotels.
3. Chance / Community-style spaces: draw a card; pass Start: collect salary; jail per the rulebook.

## Game end & scoring

- Last player not bankrupt wins, or compare total assets at an agreed time limit.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** Last player not bankrupt wins, or compare total assets at an agreed time limit.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Roll two dice and move.
3. **Setup check:** Each player takes a token and starting money (loaded onto their bank card in the electronic version).
4. **The one rule people forget:** Card-reader payments are slow — both players should check the amount.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Build only after completing a colour set.
- Agree a time limit.

## Common mistakes

- Card-reader payments are slow — both players should check the amount.
- Exact rules depend on the box.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** most board gamers
- **Not ideal for:** (rules data incomplete — read the rulebook first)

# Age of War

> Chinese name: **Age of War 战国时代** · Doc #166 · [中文原文 / Chinese doc](../166_%E6%88%98%E5%9B%BD%E6%97%B6%E4%BB%A3_Age_of_War.md) · [Open in the web app](https://codinggeoff.github.io/boardgame-shelf-rules/#doc/166)

## At a glance

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Players | 2–6 (best: 3-4) |
| Play time | 15–30 min |
| Age | 14+ |
| Complexity | ●○○○○ Very light (1/5) |
| Tags | dice, push-your-luck, quick, set collection, family |

## Where is it on the shelf?

- **Shelf code:** none on the photo — inferred area: shelf behind the wire grid, upper level, middle (no code) *(inferred)*
- **Photos:** [204000](../../photos/web/IMG20261127204000.jpg)
- **Close-up crops:** [c10](../../photos/crops/c10_204000_mid.jpg)
- Original note (Chinese): 照片 204000：上层中部（铁网后货架） → 右上 小方盒 WAR（FFG 标志）

## Overview

Reiner Knizia's feudal-Japan dice game: roll 7 dice and fill one battle line on a castle card per roll; if you cannot, lose a die and reroll. Conquer castles, steal them, and unite clans for big points.

## Setup

1. Lay all 14 castle cards (6 clans) face up in the centre.
2. Put the 7 dice nearby; choose a first player.

## How to play

1. Roll all 7 dice and choose one castle to attack (centre, or an opponent's unprotected castle).
2. After each roll you must complete exactly one battle line with matching dice; then reroll the rest.
3. If you cannot complete a line, set one die aside and reroll.
4. Complete every line to take the card; run out of dice and the attack fails. Stealing needs the extra red daimyo line.

## Game end & scoring

- The game ends when the last centre castle is taken.
- Score castles, or the higher clan value for each united clan (united clans cannot be stolen); highest total wins.

## 5-minute teaching order

1. **Goal first:** The game ends when the last centre castle is taken.
2. **What you do on a turn:** Roll all 7 dice and choose one castle to attack (centre, or an opponent's unprotected castle).
3. **Setup check:** Lay all 14 castle cards (6 clans) face up in the centre.
4. **The one rule people forget:** One castle per turn.
5. Play one open-handed practice round, then start for real.

## Strategy tips

- Fill hard lines (cavalry, archers, daimyo) first and infantry last.
- Finish clans before rivals steal from them.
- Late game: steal or take a cheap centre castle?

## Common mistakes

- One castle per turn.
- Infantry lines count total infantry; several dice can combine.
- Only united clans are protected.

## Good for / not for

- **Good for:** icebreakers; newcomers and grandparents can learn it instantly
- **Not ideal for:** no obvious bad fit

## Detailed supplement: components, worked example, scoring, FAQ, player counts, variants

> Written by the editor from the rules above as a walkthrough; numbers in the example are illustrative. The rulebook in the box always wins.

### Component checklist

From the Chinese doc (original wording):

- [ ] 14 张城堡牌，分属 6 个家族：毛利、上杉、长宗我部、岛津、德川、织田（每家 1–4 座城）
- [ ] 7 颗特制骰子，6 个面：**1 步兵、2 步兵、3 步兵、弓兵、骑兵、大名**
- [ ] 规则单

Before you start:
- Count the components against the rulebook list; sort cards by type — a missing card can break the balance.
- Check tokens, dice and score markers; coins or generic tokens can stand in for missing pieces.
- Hand out reference cards before the first game to save rulebook time.

### Worked example (illustrative)

1. 3 players: Zhang rolls 7 dice and attacks Edo (Tokugawa).
2. First roll: 2 cavalry fill the cavalry line.
3. Rerolling 5 dice completes nothing — set one die aside.
4. With 4 dice she rolls 3+2 infantry, completing the line and taking Edo.
5. Later she unites all three Tokugawa castles, scoring the clan value; they cannot be stolen.

### Scoring example

- Sum castle values, or the higher clan value for united clans; highest wins.

### Rules FAQ / rulings

- **Q: How do infantry lines work?**  
  A: Total infantry; several dice can combine.
- **Q: Can I switch targets?**  
  A: No, one castle per turn.

### Player-count differences

- 2–6; 3–4 is ideal; more players means more stealing.
- Best with: 3-4 players.

### Official variants & expansions

- No expansions; kids can drop the daimyo line for stealing as a house rule.

## Advanced guide: teaching script, time budget, phase strategy, extra FAQ

> Summarised by the editor from the rules above for teaching and improving; the rulebook wins on conflicts.

### First-game time budget

- Teaching: about 5 min (weight ●).
- Play: 15–30 min; budget about 35 min for a first game.
- Total: about 40 min.

### Teaching script (read it aloud)

> Knizia's samurai dice game. Roll 7 dice; each roll you must complete one battle line on a castle card or lose a die. Complete a castle to claim it; collect a whole clan for a bonus. Highest castle points wins.

### Strategy by phase

- **Early:** Attack easy castles.
- **Mid:** Aim to complete clans.
- **Late:** Steal unprotected castles late.

### Extra FAQ

- **Q: Steal castles?**  
  A: Yes, with an extra line (complete clans are protected).
- **Q: Out of dice?**  
  A: Fail; turn ends.
